C4i next prerequisite audit: index buffer byte length and draw count do not
prove vertex fetch bounds. Current GpuBuffer::sealWrites and shared seal state
can prohibit future host/GPU writes, but there is no typed uploaded-index
content identity or exact selected-span extrema contract. Before enabling
indexed bounded draws, define an authoritative producer binding typed bytes,
endianness/format, issued payload, immutability, selected firstIndex/count and
signed baseVertex to actual indexed vertex extents. Primitive restart must be
explicit. No native readback/CPU replay, guessed maximum, or caller-asserted
unverified bounds may substitute for that contract. Reuse c4h layout/fetch and
owner admission and core unsigned ranges; reusable typed index span algorithms
belong in core. Null Validator and Metal/Vulkan/WebGPU DiagnosticFailure remain
the current bounded indexed conclusions. Public versions0.

C4i1 contract (implementation pending): IndexInputConsumption/1 owns the
complete nonempty typed index byte content. Format is explicit Uint16/Uint32,
source byte order is explicit LittleEndian/BigEndian, and primitive restart is
explicit Disabled; Enabled or unknown enum values fail. Exact element widths
come from those unsigned integer formats. Byte extent must be divisible by
width; no padding/trailing bytes are guessed. A reusable core unsigned element
span decoder produces deterministic normalized little-endian owned bytes.
Snapshot capture runs under the preparation quota. Source destruction/mutation
cannot change the snapshot. Unknown metadata and invalid spans diagnose.

Cold selected-span analysis validates exact firstIndex/count against the
snapshot's finite element count using core unsigned ranges, then computes
min/max of only that selected span. baseVertex is int32; unsigned index values
are widened to int64 before signed addition, rejecting negative effective
vertex indices. The inclusive extrema produce a conservative contiguous fetch
range including every actual referenced vertex. This is a valid equivalent
bounds proof, not guessed geometry; gaps only make the proof stricter. Restart
sentinels are ordinary indices when restart is Disabled. Draw observations
are metadata only and never execution authorization. No index scan/readback,
allocation or source access is introduced in the recording path. Native use
requires a later backend-owned exact upload/immutable payload identity and
prepared draw binding. Full snapshot size and actual CPU quota govern cold
allocation; no unbounded hot-path cache or hidden scan is permitted.

C4i2 implementation prerequisites: private index preparation must create a

upload them once through the validated device, seal all host/GPU writes and
retain its L2 wrapper/issuing registry/native payload/allocation token. A copied
range struct or caller-owned mutable index buffer is not an authoritative
producer. Audit host write, transfer/copy and writable descriptor gates before
relying on the existing write seals. Freeze each prepared selected-span draw's
firstIndex/count/baseVertex and actual effective vertex range against that
exact index payload and immutable content. Atomic recording admission includes
all frozen owners and precedes index state mutation; native draw consumes only
prepared bounds and current Vertex/Instance bindings. No index scan or readback
in record. Uint16/Uint32, offsets, signed bases, quota/admission one-less,
identity/seal mutation and actual delayed completion/cancellation must be proven
before opening private indexed Native execution. Public versions0; Null
Validator and all GPU bounded indexed execution DiagnosticFailure remain the
current conclusions pending that evidence.

C4i2 seal audit: Metal host writeBuffer checks hostWritesSealed; public transfer
copy/upload and descriptor writing check the shared GPU seals. The three private
native transfer destination gates (buffer copy, staging upload and texture-to-
buffer copy) currently apply gpuTransferWritesSealed only when
usesRecordingRetention_ is true. This conditional cannot prove immutable index
content against another private command without retention enabled. Establish
an unconditional canonical destination seal gate (with negative tests for both
command modes) before authorizing any immutable indexed upload consumer. Also
prove the dedicated index buffer's IndexBuffer-only usage blocks writable
storage descriptors. No indexed execution gate has been opened.

C4i2a contract before implementation: monotonic GpuBuffer GPU transfer write

reject before admission/encoding when the issuing registry observes the seal.
The existing path-specific stable resource-invalid diagnostics remain valid;
no owner counts or destination bytes change on rejection. Reads of sealed
buffers remain allowed. Null/Vulkan/WebGPU existing L2 seal gates are Validator/
Native/Native enforcement respectively where real runtime exists, absent native

Bounded indexed execution is still DiagnosticFailure for all GPU backends.
Required evidence: both native command modes for all three destinations, exact

C4i2b/c contract before implementation: private cold index preparation accepts
borrowed typed bytes plus explicit format/order/restart metadata, copies and
normalizes under the actual core quota, creates an IndexBuffer-only buffer on
the exact issuing Metal device/registry, uploads those exact owned bytes once
and calls sealWrites. The prepared root owns the L2 wrapper through core
shareUnique and retains its canonical payload/device/object/token/registry and
provider projections. Hot observation checks wrapper id/descriptor/full extent,
all three seals and canonical/native control-block identities; no byte scans.
IndexBuffer-only usage independently forbids writable storage descriptors and
transfer destination usage. Preparation must fail on native/allocator/budget/
upload/registry/identity errors; no placeholder data or successful empty upload.

Cold prepared indexed draw plans alone compute a selected span from explicit
index binding byte offset (element aligned), firstIndex/count, signed baseVertex,
instanceCount/firstInstance. The byte offset identifies part of the same full
normalized upload; exact selected first/count are checked before multiplication.
The immutable plan captures effective vertex bounds and native index byte offset
and all index/provider/resource owners. A range struct grants no authority.
Recording observes the exact current provider/registry/quota, descriptor/push
state and all Vertex/Instance bindings. It validates their full actual ranges
against the frozen indexed bounds, preflights plan plus its full owner union
atomically, then emits one native indexed draw from the frozen parameters.
No raw bounded index binding/draw entry opens; no hot index scan/upload/readback,
shader compilation, new pipeline or default allocation is permitted. Rejection
must preserve prior owner/native draw state. Provider/pass resets apply existing
vertex freshness rules; completion/cancellation release original frozen owners.

C4j prerequisite audit: graphicsBufferFacts captures outer buffer argument
layout. An argument buffer's pointer table layout cannot prove the pointed-to
buffer's required byte span/alignment. The existing bounded texture collector
already enumerates exact root slot/argumentIndex plus array argument-index
stride; reuse that shape for typed buffer pointees. Native MTLPointerType exposes
access/alignment/dataSize (sizeof pointee), with MTLArrayType.elementPointerType,
arrayLength and argumentIndexStride. Establish exact selected-stage logical
mapping/native pointee facts and immutable captures before any readonly array
execution. Missing/zero/unknown/writable/nested ambiguous facts fail; no inferred
stride or copying the outer argument buffer encodedLength as a pointee span.
Reuse core ranges, stage capture validation and complete canonical owners.
Null Validator; bounded readonly arrays on all GPU backends DiagnosticFailure
until native data/ownership/quota/lifetime evidence. Public versions0 and
separate indirect/writable/RenderDoc/platform/Production gates remain open.

C4j1/1 defines a separate per-selected-stage pointee fact domain. Keys are the
actual root buffer slot and argument index; values retain native array count,
argument-index stride, element ordinal, access/type, alignment and sizeof(T)
span. Direct buffer facts continue to describe the outer MTLArgument only.
Neither outer dataSize nor argument encoder encodedLength authorizes a pointee
range. Query storage is bounded by logical buffer cardinality before pipeline
creation; query capacity/duplicate/unknown/zero/writable/nested argument-buffer
facts fail closed with stable diagnostics. Exact indices use a reusable checked
strided-index range; no source-name inference or guessed stride is allowed.
Range capture observes each selected stage independently, resolves one exact
fact, validates offset alignment and the complete sizeof(T) range, and freezes
all fact fields. Missing/unavailable reflection cannot select another ABI.
Null validates range metadata only; Metal query is Native on actual supported
reflection APIs, otherwise DiagnosticFailure. Vulkan/WebGPU and absent Metal
runtime do not execute this private domain. All readonly array execution stays
DiagnosticFailure until c4j2 proves per-element owners/data/lifetime. This query
contract adds no public capability; public versions remain0.

SingleEntryShaderArtifact/2 is restricted to the explicit
WgslBufferArrayDiagnostic/1 publication policy. Source/SPIR-V/MSL/reflection
remain required hash-sealed files; the WGSL role must instead declare
DiagnosticFailure and SHADER-WGSL-BUFFER-ARRAY-UNSUPPORTED, with no path/hash/
bytes. The compiler must actually return only Slang E56105 and authoritative
selected SPIR-V descriptor metadata must confirm a buffer array. Any other
compiler failure is fatal. Reflection must agree: unavailable WebGPU target,
empty WGSL path/mappings and the same stable diagnostic. v1 continues requiring
all five artifacts. Loader accessors expose target availability/diagnostic;
absent WGSL never grants execution. Negative artifact tests cover unsupported
version/policy/status/id, forged reflection/semantics and stale WGSL files.

WgslBufferArrayDiagnostic/2 additionally handles a successful Slang WGSL
emission containing native buffer descriptor arrays (binding_array or the
compiler's pre-compaction array spelling). Explicit diagnostic opt-in,
selected active Slang buffer-array declarations, exact SPIRV-Reflect descriptor
cardinalities and matching emitted WGSL names/capacities are mandatory. Missing,
duplicate, scalarized or changed-capacity declarations require contract review;
they cannot be classified from an arbitrary parser error. The successful but
unsupported target is deleted before ordinary WGSL transformations/reflection.
The version4 publication uses WgslBufferArrayDiagnostic/2 with
SHADER-WGSL-BUFFER-ARRAY-ABI-UNAVAILABLE and empty WGSL bytes/path/mappings.
Actual compiler E56105 retains the version1 diagnostic policy. Unrelated compiler
errors remain fatal. This is a compiler refusal, never scalar expansion or an
execution capability: Null validates unavailable artifacts, Vulkan/Metal consume
their independently sealed targets, WebGPU rejects native buffer-array ABI.
Validation (2026-10-09): assertion-enabled MinGW compiler/artifact/vertex/index
CTest5/5 passed167.00 seconds; matrix/retention/Null smoke3/3 passed4.27 seconds.
Real Slang emits the pre-compaction array spelling; the existing descriptor-array

### Metal shared command substrate CPU ownership clarification

The existing shared native command queue and AtomicCapacityBudget are two
once-only process/backend owners. Their native command-buffer ceiling remains
the existing version-controlled kMetalNativeCommandBufferCapacity, consumed by
both queue creation and reservation. Their two CPU control blocks use the core
process allocator and must not inherit an unrelated per-package/preparation
quota. This was already explicit at private prepared command factory call sites;
the same scope now belongs to each shared initializer so ordinary first use
cannot retain a caller quota until process exit. No per-command/root allocation
escapes its quota, no capacity/value/lease change and no new GPU work follow.
The once-only two-owner graph bounds this shared CPU initialization; it is not
a per-root resource or an unbounded cache. Null/Vulkan/WebGPU do not use this
Metal shared initializer and retain their existing private payload failure gates.

Cold consumer tests deliberately first create a rejected ordinary transfer
command under a package quota, then destroy all scoped roots and the device.
The quota must return to zero while the process shared queue/capacity persist;
all scoped command capacity leases must be released. Fast shared contracts,
Null hidden frame and existing actual native command/retention regressions remain
required. This ownership clarification grants no native synchronization,
consumer plan, execution or Platform/Production acceptance.

preflights199 child reference slots and123 retained unique owners, including

readback, independent consumer cold owners, journal and encoder slots. No
ordinary recording entry is legal on this exclusive producer command.

Only a complete15-operation sequence can finish/submit. Unsubmitted cancel,

states and releases the original producer fault lease, allowing a fresh root
for the unconsumed tuple. At commit admission the producer generation becomes

checks actual original command and wait-prologue status, marks fault readback
only on success, releases the lease and publishes atomic Completed/Failed.
Caller completion flags and CPU expectations cannot change root/lease status.

## WebBindlessExceptionLowering/1 (browser baseline prerequisite)

The C++ CPU quota, object lifetime and deterministic allocation diagnostic
contracts apply equally to a real Emscripten payload. Native Null/Vulkan/Metal
builds use their existing native C++ exception ABI. WebGPU browser artifacts
must use one consistent C++ exception representation for every archive and
executable: Emscripten JavaScript exception lowering (`-fexceptions`, with
`-sDISABLE_EXCEPTION_CATCHING=0` at link). This preserves `std::bad_alloc`
catching and C++ RAII cleanup used by bounded preparation. It is a build-time
representation choice and grants no GPU capability or completion authority.

The existing browser RHI fixture and runtime use Asyncify for asynchronous GPU
callbacks. Emscripten4.0.15 explicitly rejects Asyncify combined with native
WebAssembly exception lowering (`-fwasm-exceptions`); the current combination
fails in Binaryen Asyncify at link. Do not disable catching, fabricate successful
callback results or switch async execution at runtime. Remove the incompatible
Wasm exception flag consistently from both the CMake source generator and its
checked-in generated graph, while keeping explicit exception catching. Existing
ScriptVM exception semantics use result/unwind state rather than the C++ ABI.

Acceptance requires actual target compilation/link and browser RHI data/fault/
completion smoke, plus an assertion-enabled Wasm core test exercising exhausted
CPU quota throws/catches and cleanup. Preserve quick BindlessCross/retention and
Null hidden-frame checks. Browser-driver allocations, complete ordinary command
retention, RenderDoc visual evidence, mobile lifecycle and platform/production
acceptance remain independent gates. Status is Contract Complete for this
build representation; Backend/Platform validation requires the stated runs.

Web baseline verified 2026-10-02 with Emscripten4.0.15, pinned Emdawn
v20250807.221415 and Chrome154 mac-arm64. Both actual indirect feature modes
pass full public/private compute/graphics/packet/fault/lifetime/loss smoke;
Wasm bounded allocation throw/catch/cleanup and shared BindlessCross pass.
Native quick/retention/Null hidden-frame also pass. Contract fixture methods
and separate public graphics setup/frame scopes retain original owners while
reducing debug Wasm stack pressure; ordinary65536 and Asyncify16384 byte limits
remain unchanged. An authorization-owned package alias must be rejected; the
negative fixture now preserves that rejection and reuses the original canonical
package owner for its independent authorization. The browser owns the imported
GPUDevice: device-loss injection destroys that exact JS object and observes its
real lost promise, since pinned Emdawn imported handles have no native lost
future. This is fixture injection, not a native completion substitute. Evidence
webgpu-baseline-report.yaml and scoped-baseline logs/XML. Scoped Backend Validated
for existing data/lifetime paths; RenderDoc visual acceptance, Platform Validated,
Production Ready and public retention capability remain open.

## WebBindlessFaultColdCompletion/1

L2 fault-report semantics and the original snapshot submission lease are
unchanged: report Pending until actual queue success followed by successful
native mapping; exact four-byte fault data produces Clean/Faulted. Native queue
or map failure produces ReadbackFailed; destruction before submission produces
Abandoned. A report and its latch lease belong to exactly one command submission.
No reusable/reset result, replacement lease, CPU completion or Dummy buffer is
admitted. This WebGPU adapter publishes terminal status after lease retirement,
so its visible terminal report cannot leave the original snapshot in flight.
Actual successful queue completion records the existing CopySrc prior state,
matching the Vulkan/Metal fault-copy lowering; Null remains a validator.

| Backend | This private preparation change | L2/capability effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Null | Validator only; existing fault statuses remain non-native | No promotion |
| Vulkan | Existing native fault/readback lowering unchanged | No promotion |
| Metal | Existing native completion/closed consumer lowering unchanged | No promotion |
| WebGPU | Native queue -> map callback; cold bounded fault slots | No promotion |

The caller's existing maxBindlessFaultReadbacks is the sole slot count. Cold
command creation owns that many unique callback records, immutable one-shot
shared result objects and native CopyDst|MapRead buffers of sizeof(uint32_t).
It must prepare the full declared capacity or return deterministic failure;
no record-time allocation, dynamic growth or temporary fallback is allowed.
Native bytes are exactly count*sizeof(uint32_t); CPU storage is the core-PMR
slot/record vector plus core-owned callback and shared-result control blocks.
This private cold scratch does not advertise full CommandRetentionBudget/1 or
claim complete ordinary command GPU/CPU byte admission. All public versions
remain0. Future admission must charge this explicit graph to the full budget.

Recording consumes one cold slot only after existing device/latch/clear/capacity/
duplicate gates. It records the exact native copy and transfers the original
snapshot lease as before. Finish and no-wait submit allocate no core object;
submit moves the already prepared unique callback owner into the queue callback,
which transfers the same state to map callback. Command destruction cannot
abandon submitted results. Unused cold slots and unsubmitted pending slots clean
up their real buffers; partial cold allocation/native failure must release the
entire graph and reservation. Stable failures are
RHI-WEBGPU-COMMAND-PREPARATION-ALLOCATION,
RHI-WEBGPU-BINDLESS-FAULT-READBACK-CREATION-FAILED and existing capacity/duplicate
ids. Cold retry requires budget/capacity correction or native device recovery;
failed/abandoned results are never reused. Missing callbacks stay Pending and
retain authority; device loss uses existing actual loss/queue/map failure paths.

Acceptance: real Wasm cold quota failure and cleanup; exhausted quota schedule/
finish/no-wait submit allocation0; zero/one capacity, duplicate/cancel checks;
caller drops command/root before real callback, both Clean/Faulted exact GPU
readbacks and actual device loss; shared quick four-backend/Null hidden-frame
and both browser feature modes. Timestamp/upload/ordinary texture callback
allocations are separate remaining adapters. Status Contract Complete for this
subset; implementation and Backend Validated require those tests. RenderDoc and
platform/production acceptance remain independent.

This source inventory grants no native execution or capability. Reuse the
existing CommandRetentionBudget/1 independent maxResourceOwners, maxQueryPools,
maxTimestampWrites/maxPipelineStatisticsWrites and maxCpuMetadataBytes. Reuse
CommandRetentionStorage preflightResourceProjection/retainResourceProjection,
preflightTimestamp/admitTimestamp, seal/submit/retire and the existing core
CompletionRetentionPool/AtomicCapacityBudget/CPU quota fixtures. A backend
adapter must enumerate exact registered core owners and stable native payloads;
count is not a byte budget, driver objects do not replace original wrappers or
allocation-release tokens, and a callback must retain the sealed graph through
actual completion or explicit loss/quarantine.

| Existing path | Concrete remaining gap | Required next lowering |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Ordinary resource/render attachments | Impl retainedTextureViews/retainedTextures/retainedRenderBundles and descriptor vectors can grow while recording; general graph is command-owned | Exact registered immutable owner projection, bounded atomic preflight and native completion root |
| Descriptor binding | boundDescriptorSets owns nested dynamicOffsets and active descriptor tables/groups/layouts are rebuilt during recording | Cold reflected layout/payload/offset preparation, exact registered owner enumeration; no count-derived binding guess |
| Timestamp recording | recordTimestamp grows retainedTimingPools and recordedTimestampQueries, then marks pool state | Preflight original pool owner/index/state plus independent query/writes/resource capacity before native mutation |
| Timestamp finish/submit | finish creates per-query resolve/readback buffers and appends pending records; submit allocates WebGpuTimestampReadbackCallbackState | Cold native buffers, one-shot callback records and stable query storage; actual queue/map proof and explicit Future semantics |
| Upload/staging submit | submit creates/reserves a temporary retainedBuffers vector and transfers raw upload handles to runtime callback paths | Preserve exact original staging/resource/allocation owners under explicit cold slot/byte/completion admission |
| Timing/texture readback | runtime map helpers and pendingTextureReadbacks are outside the b4b fault graph; recording retains mutable label/format strings and native destinations | Separate explicit opt-in bounded readback/timing preparation with deterministic diagnostic failure for unsupported paths |

The source references are webgpu_native_command_payload_impl.h ownership vectors,
webgpu_native_commands.cpp recordTimestamp/finish/submit/readbackTexture and
webgpu_native_runtime.cpp timing/staging/upload/queue callback helpers. The
existing fault and producer closed subsets do not authorize these graphs.

Next c2 must start with a narrowly opted-in private ordinary transfer domain:
original registry-issued resources and allocation charges are projected into
common storage under a real CPU quota before native recording. Enumerate all
children deterministically, including staging and readback owners. Completion
moves the sealed original root into a cold callback reservation, preserving
logical owner charges with caller root/command retained or dropped. Every
unsupported descriptor/render/query/texture-readback path must reject before
native mutation. No inferred limit, empty payload, CPU completion or accidental
legacy inheritance may fill a missing lowering. Null remains a validator;
Vulkan/Metal retain their current exact contracts and WebGPU public retention
version remains0/DiagnosticFailure. The subsequent query domain must consume
existing maxQueryPools/maxTimestampWrites, not a new guessed native count.

The same quick shared retention/BindlessCross/WebGPU contract and Null hidden-
frame paths remain available, alongside both real browser feature modes. The
inventory is complete as an audit; adapter Contract/Backend/Platform/Production
acceptance remains pending c2/c3 and subsequent supported domains.

## WebOrdinaryTransferManifest/1 (M4e5b4c2)

This explicit backend-private Prototype opt-in consumes existing L2 copy
descriptors; it does not enable public CommandRetentionBudget capabilities.
Before encoder creation the caller declares an immutable manifest of registered
buffer/texture IDs, an independent maxTransferResources, maxResourceOwners,
maxCpuMetadataBytes quota and maxRetainedResourceBytes. The last bound measures
the original allocator ledger charges (ResourceAllocation.estimatedSizeBytes),
not a guessed physical driver allocation or count-derived byte allowance.
No new staging/scratch GPU allocation is authorized by this manifest.

Each independent texture has two projected roles: original registered native
payload and original allocation-release token. Each buffer additionally retains
its original GPU write-seal state; dropping the wrapper cannot undo a seal.
Aliased/shared allocation tokens are unsupported in this version. The L2 wrappers are unique
owners in the existing WebGPU device API; their destruction unregisters the ID
but cannot release a payload/ledger charge already captured by this manifest.
The native device is retained by the separately budgeted completion owner.
Observation takes a nonblocking try-lock and snapshots all requested entries
atomically without copying labels. Missing owners, wrong resource
kind, alias/changed native payload or token, foreign device, sealed destination,
unsupported surface texture and registry contention reject deterministically.
Hot native recording consumes only manifest IDs and must match the prepared
payload/owner/handle facts; registry or wrapper lifetime is no longer required.

Preparation uses core allocation and CommandRetentionStorage, covers the
entire declared graph or fails and releases it, and cannot grow. Duplicate IDs
are rejected. Resource slots, unique owner slots, CPU quota and ledger bytes
are independent gates. Cancellation abandons the same original storage.
Buffer copies, buffer-to-texture, texture-to-buffer and texture-to-texture
copies consume registered resources. An explicit registered CpuToGpu buffer
can be the staging source; a registered GpuToCpu buffer can be a copy target.
Host-byte queue.writeTexture, generated staging, descriptor/render/compute,
query, bindless and automatic texture mapping are outside this version and
must reject before mutation. General public/legacy factories stay separate.
Version1's native texture domain is RGBA8Unorm, single-sample, single physical
layer, 2D, explicit single-mip ranges and origin zero. Other texture domains
reject deterministically; they do not inherit legacy lowering. The shared
TextureTransferValidation extracts the existing extent/layout validator and
uses core StridedIndexRange/unsigned-range validation for the exact last row:
2x2 RGBA8 with256-byte pitch requires264 bytes, not16. Overflow rejects.

Native submit transfers the sealed original graph into a cold completion-pool
ticket before queue acceptance, then registers an already allocated callback.
Actual queue Success retires it; unproven completion closes admission and
quarantines it until the same native device's actual lost promise invalidates
the pool. Future.id is not completion/registration proof in pinned Emdawn.
Missing callback remains pending. The existing device-loss observer is shared
per prepared completion owner; no per-command permanent observer is added.

| Backend | Private manifest lowering | Public retention capability |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Null | Existing shared storage validator; no native completion proof | version0 / DiagnosticFailure |
| Vulkan | Existing native retention domain; this WebGPU-private request has no Vulkan entry point | version0 / DiagnosticFailure |
| Metal | Existing native retention domain; this WebGPU-private request has no Metal entry point | version0 / DiagnosticFailure |
| WebGPU | Explicit Prototype manifest, real encoder/queue/completion payload required | version0 / DiagnosticFailure |

Stable diagnostics use RHI-WEBGPU-TRANSFER-* (manifest, logical/native/allocation
owner missing, identity changed, count/byte limit, registry busy, preparation
allocation, unsupported command, completion closed/unproven). Retry requires
correct metadata/ownership/budget or device recovery; a sealed/submitted root
cannot be reused. Contract tests cover gates and negative paths; native browser
data/lifetime tests are mandatory for Backend Validated. Null/source checks
never prove native completion. RenderDoc diagnostic remains
RHI-RENDERDOC-TOOL-UNAVAILABLE here; no visual/platform/production acceptance.

Counts, ledger bytes, measured exact/one-less CPU quota, huge container counts,

An injected unproven ownership result closes admission and keeps the original

## WebTimestampCommandPointGate/1 (M4e5b4c3a)

L2 writeTimestamp records an existing query index at its explicit command point,
including a point inside an active render/compute pass. A device's
timestamp-query feature alone is insufficient. This gate separately observes
the exact browser device's feature, encoder.writeTimestamp,
GPURenderPassEncoder.writeTimestamp, GPUComputePassEncoder.writeTimestamp,
encoder.resolveQuerySet/copyBufferToBuffer and GPUBuffer map/read/unmap API.
Native creation/recording can be enabled only if the complete existing command-
point lowering exists and native query/resolve/readback preparation succeeds.
Missing feature yields RHI-WEBGPU-TIMESTAMP-FEATURE-UNAVAILABLE; missing command
or readback API yields RHI-WEBGPU-TIMESTAMP-COMMAND-API-UNAVAILABLE or
RHI-WEBGPU-TIMESTAMP-READBACK-API-UNAVAILABLE. They are stable Degraded decisions
with executable=false, independently of browser/backend/OS names.

| Backend | Command-point conclusion for this gate |
| --- | --- |
| Null | Equivalent validator only; no native Available/completion claim |
| Vulkan | Existing Native timestamp command-point lowering; no implementation change here |
| Metal | Existing queried sampling-point gate; Apple M4 draw=0/dispatch=0/stage=1 remains Degraded/default rejection (RHI-METAL-TIMESTAMP-SAMPLING-POINT-UNSUPPORTED), as recorded in M4e5b2a |
| WebGPU | Native only with all API facts and query/readback payload; missing facts are Degraded/default rejection with the stable ids above |

WebGPU must reject the incomplete lowering. Modern
pass-descriptor timestampWrites does not authorize moving an arbitrary L2
command point to a pass boundary. An equivalent lowering needs a separate
semantic proof/version before implementation. No empty pass, CPU value or
successful Dummy query may bypass this gate. Public command retention remains0.

Pinned Emdawn map facts from webgpu.cpp: valid AllowSpontaneous registers a
MapAsyncEvent retaining the original buffer; an already-pending map request can
deliver Error synchronously from SetFutureReady before MapAsync returns.
Callback ownership must therefore transfer before the native call, and caller
code must not access or free the callback state afterwards based on Future.id.
MapAsyncEvent sets mapped/unmapped state before invoking the callback. Invalid
mode returns null Future without registration; all adapter modes are explicit
and validated constants. Missing callback keeps original ownership Pending.
Queue Success is distinct from successful map/read; query Available requires
actual native readback, while failure/loss must use the original graph.

The forthcoming c3b adapter must cold-prepare maxTimestampWrites exact resolve/
readback buffer pairs and callbacks under independent maxQueryPools, resource
owners, CPU quota and explicit native bytes. Existing query-pool mutex/state
gates must also have nonblocking bounded preflight before hot native mutation.
Until a command-point payload is proven, this gate is contract/capability work
only; it does not complete bounded native query execution or claim Backend/
Platform/Production validation for a query adapter.

API diagnostic above. Every independently missing fact rejects in the quick
contract fixture. Eight native/Null/WebGPU regression tests and four Wasm/
browser tests pass. Evidence timestamp-gate-report.yaml and timestamp-gate
logs/XML. Contract Complete / Prototype Complete for this gate only; no query

### Existing map callback handoff prerequisite (M4e5b4c3a1)

The prior timestamp/timing/texture/runtime-smoke map registrations kept unique
ownership during MapAsync and inferred cleanup from Future.id.
Pinned MapAsyncEvent can invoke its Error callback before returning when a map
is already pending; the callback destroys that state. Registration must release
the original unique state and exchange owned native handles before calling the
API. Only the actual callback may read/unmap/destroy/release them afterwards.
The explicit validated AllowSpontaneous mode registers the event; Future.id is
neither completion proof nor a safe post-call state-lifetime observation.
Missing callback leaves ownership pending and cannot publish Available.

This repair consumes the existing WebGPU native event contract and changes no
L2 timestamp semantics or API capability. Null/Vulkan/Metal retain their
existing lowerings. Asynchronous mapped data, synchronous native pending-map
Error, original CPU/native-owner retirement and quick/Null/browser regression
evidence are required. It adds no guessed budget and grants no bounded command
retention capability: legacy callback allocations and general submission
ownership remain in the c3b/remaining-adapter inventory.

Verified 2026-10-02: these four map registrations now release the original
unique record and exchange command/runtime-owned buffers before registration.
No map Future.id cleanup remains. Native Success unmaps even if mapped-range
read fails; non-success never unmaps. The existing core unique-allocation entry
and explicit callback mode are reused without a new allocator or payload.

Both real browser modes execute native pending-map requests followed by the
actual timing and texture helpers. Emdawn delivers the second request's Error
synchronously; the production callback's native Destroy synchronously aborts
the first request. The timing state quota is fully retired before return; no
timestamp sample becomes Available. A separate asynchronous texture request
verifies the specification-defined zero-initialized R32Float bytes, scalar and
channel statistics and original callback-state quota retirement. These are map
data/lifetime proofs, never synthetic query execution evidence. The pinned
pending-map error returns a registered nonzero Future: this test does not
claim to reproduce the old zero-Future cleanup branch or a double-free. Source
guards enforce handoff independently of those observable successful cleanups.

The initial synchronous texture probe under a callback-only quota failed in
the existing global sample deque's allocation. Failure log/XML is preserved as
map-handoff-initial-failure-*; this queue has no independent admitted byte/count
budget or exhausted-callback failure containment. The synchronous texture test
therefore tests map lifecycle without calling that deque bounded. The
asynchronous state-only quota applies only at registration; queue storage is
explicitly outside it. This limitation requires the independent c4a adapter,
not an increased/guessed command quota or a Production Ready claim.

Eight native quick/Null/WebGPU regressions and four Wasm/browser tests pass.
Evidence map-handoff-report.yaml and map-handoff logs/XML. Contract Complete /

### Remaining ordinary adapter audit (M4e5b4c4a)

WebGPU ordinary descriptors retain copies of layout/writes/native binding
vectors and generation tickets; native bind-group preparation builds additional
payload/offset vectors while recording. The resource registry can already
atomically project original buffer/texture/allocation/seal graphs, but it has no
descriptor-set frozen revision/owner projection. A bounded adapter must prove
that graph before consuming descriptor data, including original generation,
parent view/sampler owners and reflected physical binding metadata.

Raw recordBufferCopy and the other raw transfer entry points consume resource
payload references without retaining their original core owners/allocation
tokens in Impl. Keeping only a generic command payload therefore cannot prove
their L2 lifetime/accounting. Native execution of a new ordinary envelope must
require the full original resource graph, with
RHI-WEBGPU-ORDINARY-COMPLETION-RESOURCE-GRAPH-UNPROVEN for missing proof. The
registered transfer manifest already proves its closed resource subset; it
does not authorize generic descriptors/views/samplers/raw transfers. Complete
those cold projections in c4c before generic execution integration.

Runtime staging/upload/queue helpers still allocate callback records after
submission and use unbounded unreclaimed vectors for zero-Future guesses.
The pinned explicit valid callback mode registers WorkDoneEvent before return;
Future.id alone is neither failure nor completion. Existing generic completion
and staging callbacks ignore WorkDoneStatus when publishing completion/serial;
the next contract must gate those publications on actual Success, quarantine
unproven original graphs and bind retirement to the exact device's loss. These
existing paths receive no bounded retention or NativeSync promotion from c2
or c3a1. The global sample deque and mutable readback labels/pending records
likewise require independent preparation and exhausted-publication failure
diagnostics. No new adapter may inherit them as an implicit fallback.

## WebOrdinaryCompletionPreparation/1 (M4e5b4c4a / c4b1)

This backend-private Prototype contract covers original callback metadata
preparation, separately from resource graph admission and execution. The
caller must explicitly opt in to version1 and independently declare a maximum
pending-record count and aggregate CPU metadata bytes backed by the same
CpuMemoryQuota. Neither the logical frame count, resource count, staging bytes,
bindless quotas nor backend/OS name supplies those limits. No native staging,
scratch, query or resource memory is allocated by this preparation domain.

Cold preparation retains the original factory-issued command shared owner,
its original optional completion-state owner and immutable native queue/device/
recording identity in a core CompletionRetentionPool reservation. Factory
issuance must compare pointer and control-block identity; an alias to the same
object with another control block is not an original owner. An existing command
cannot be admitted twice concurrently. Missing/foreign/unissued command,
device-loss observation, count/quota/version/opt-in or identity proof rejects
before any GPU work or completion-state mutation. Independent descriptor,
resource/view/sampler/allocation graphs are not replaced by retaining only the
command object; they remain mandatory in c4c before public budget acceptance.

The owner cold-installs observation of its exact native device's actual lost
promise. The observer and every reservation are charged to the explicit
metadata quota. Dropping an unsubmitted reservation cancels its original graph;
failure rollback returns every partially prepared core owner. Once submitted,
an unproven completion quarantines that same graph and closes admission. Only
actual Success can publish completion/advance staging/retire native uploads;
actual exact-device loss may invalidate quarantined graphs without publishing
success. Missing callback leaves the original graph Pending. These last
execution obligations belong to c4b2; c4b1 has no native submit/publication API.

| Backend | Preparation / execution conclusion |
| --- | --- |
| Null | Equivalent validator for count/owner/quota/state gates; never native payload or completion evidence |
| Vulkan | Existing native command/fence graph unchanged; native fence result, independently proven resource ownership and original retirement remain required |
| Metal | Existing completion pool/actual command-buffer callback lowering unchanged; original root stays quarantined on unproven result until exact terminal invalidation |
| WebGPU | c4b1 private metadata preparation only; execution is DiagnosticFailure/unavailable until c4b2 consumes the original graph. Current legacy generic callbacks remain outside this contract |

Stable preparation ids are RHI-WEBGPU-ORDINARY-COMPLETION-VERSION,
...-OPT-IN, ...-BUDGET, ...-QUOTA, ...-ALLOCATION, ...-NATIVE-UNAVAILABLE,
...-LOSS-OBSERVER, ...-ORIGINAL-COMMAND, ...-DUPLICATE, ...-CAPACITY and ...-CLOSED.
Recovery requires correct version/opt-in/explicit quota or a new device/owner;
closed/quarantined owners cannot silently reopen. Unsupported metadata domains
must be diagnosed before execution, never filled with Dummy payloads.

Quick contract/unit and Null hidden-frame remain mandatory, along with actual
browser cold native-command and exact-device-loss ownership tests in both
feature modes. Injected unproven outcomes prove negative ownership gates only;
they cannot claim a native error or successful submission. Contract Complete /
Prototype Complete may describe c4b1, but Backend Validated for execution needs
actual queue payload/data/completion evidence in c4b2. Public retention version0,
NativeSync=false and RenderDoc/platform/production gates stay independent.

c4c reuse review: DescriptorObject already supplies contentRevision and a
LifetimeEpoch ticket; DescriptorSet::write commits the revision only after a
successful backend write. LifetimeEpoch explicitly requires externally
serialized mutation/destruction/observation, so a new snapshot must not invent
thread safety by reading the same mutable vectors twice. Reuse this contract
and core::LifetimeObserver, the existing DescriptorConsumptionContract scalar
reflection projection and Metal's ordinary descriptor observation pattern.

frozen layout/write/dynamic-offset revision contract; native payload retention
alone does not prove that missing mapping. The next resource/descriptor adapter
must freeze and validate those facts before c4b2 executes generic work.

## WebOrdinaryResourceProjection/1 (M4e5b4c4c1)

This cold backend-private Prototype projection precedes descriptor execution.
It accepts registered buffers, registered texture views with a live registered
parent texture, and registered native samplers. It reuses the transfer
observation for original buffer/texture/native allocation/seal ownership and
CommandRetentionStorage for bounded owner storage. Mutation, destruction and
observation are externally serialized; registry capture uses one nonblocking
try-lock. No labels are copied into the projection.

Version1 and explicit Prototype opt-in are mandatory. Manifest entries, native
view objects, native sampler objects, retained buffer/texture ledger bytes,
retained owner slots and CPU metadata bytes are independent caller limits.
Only actual registered allocation ledger charges supply resource bytes; two
views of one original texture count its charge once. Sharing must agree on
the exact native payload and allocation pointer/control block, ledger size and
device. A reused allocation with a different backing payload is rejected.
Samplers and views are opaque native state objects with no WebGPU addressable
backing allocation size query: their explicit object-count budgets cover those
objects, not invented GPU byte estimates. This does not establish an aggregate
driver-memory budget or public retention capability.

Capture freezes native view identity, full creation range/format/dimension/
aspect/usage metadata, original parent identity/allocation and actual sampler
device/object identity. View usage is explicitly lowered from resolved L2
usage rather than relying on a native default. Missing native metadata,
unregistered/dead parent, substituted original allocation/control block, count
or quota rejects atomically. Hot matches/find allocate zero and compare only
the original frozen graph; dropping wrappers cannot retire its allocation
until the projection releases it. Native group/reflection/descriptor revisions
and offsets are deliberately not executable through this projection.

| Backend | Conclusion for this prerequisite |
| --- | --- |
| Null | Equivalent count/quota/missing-owner validator only; no native proof |
| Vulkan | Existing native ownership unchanged; no WebGPU projection entry |
| Metal | Existing original descriptor/resource observer unchanged; no new lowering |
| WebGPU | Private Prototype cold resource projection; generic descriptor execution remains DiagnosticFailure (RHI-WEBGPU-ORDINARY-COMPLETION-RESOURCE-GRAPH-UNPROVEN) until c4c2/c4b2 |

## WebOrdinaryDescriptorObservation/1 (M4e5b4c4c2a)

Cold preparation freezes the original externally serialized DescriptorObject
lifetime ticket/content revision, arena generation, exact scalar layout, factory
logical group owner/control block, binding writes and canonical dynamic-offset
order. It independently bounds binding/write/offset counts and snapshot CPU
metadata; c4c1 resource counts/bytes/CPU quota remain separate. Distinct resource
IDs reuse BoundedSequence; original resources must match every frozen binding
owner. Layout names/labels are absent from this observation. It reuses exact
descriptor binding validation, LifetimeObserver and c4c1 observations instead of
reading mutable vectors twice to infer concurrency safety.

New-use validation rejects revision mutation, expired descriptor, stale arena,
logical group/native resource owner substitution and dead public resource IDs.
Registry liveness uses one try-lock and existing original observation capture
into stack metadata, with no allocation or label copies. A recorded/retained
graph survives wrapper drop, but dead IDs cannot authorize new native binding.
Hot validation reports stable const diagnostic IDs and allocates0 on success
or failure. The logical payload is not a physical executable group. Descriptor
tables/binding arrays remain explicit DiagnosticFailure in this private domain.

Dynamic offsets are frozen in binding order. This prerequisite permits only
zero offsets: current generic native entries use WHOLE_SIZE, which resolves
the full buffer at group creation. A nonzero dynamic offset cannot safely be
treated as a reduced suffix binding range. Until c4c2b proves an exact reflected
range contract, reject with RHI-WEBGPU-ORDINARY-DESCRIPTOR-OFFSET-RANGE-UNPROVEN.
No guessed alignment, reflected minimum size or silent zeroing is allowed.

Native attachment view calls must provide RenderAttachment usage from the
render-pass intent and the validated target format. Native view creation rejects
unresolved None usage; it cannot inherit a behavioral default from WebGPU.
Null/Vulkan/Metal attachment lowering is unchanged. Existing actual browser
color/resolve/depth and sampled data regressions remain required; RenderDoc
unavailability prevents visual/Platform/Production acceptance of this mapping.

Null provides budget/missing-native validator evidence only. Vulkan and Metal
existing descriptor observation/lowering are unchanged. WebGPU this private

## WebOrdinaryPipelineOwnership/1 (M4e5b4c4c2b1)

This prerequisite establishes original native pipeline ownership before a
physical bind-group adapter. The four actual generic/bindless Compute/Graphics
factories alone can issue its construction key, after creating the actual
pipeline and original pipeline layout on the imported device. Issuance keeps
that device reference and freezes native object/layout identity, common binding
semantics, emitted physical binding shapes and logical-to-physical remaps.
No caller-populated cache, backend/OS name, raw handle, alias control block or
unissued makeShared object can replace the issued native contract.

Its source CpuMemoryQuota is captured during the synchronous real factory
scope. Preparation requires the explicit original metadata limit to equal that
quota's limit, independently of snapshot metadata bytes and mapping/binding/
group count limits. Source shader/module construction scratch may be charged
to that original quota and retired in the cold factory; retaining the original
native object does not reconstruct its metadata elsewhere. Missing original
quota fails closed in this new adapter. Existing legacy factory callers are
not silently admitted or promoted. Device/pipeline/layout/driver opaque objects
have explicit object counts rather than invented GPU byte sizes; addressable
resource/range/upload budgets remain independently mandatory before execution.

Cold observations freeze the issued original owner and complete remap/shape
facts. Hot validation compares the current mutable legacy caches against the
issued facts and original native identity without allocations or native queries.
Original native group-layout references may be queried only in subsequent cold
physical preparation, against this same issued pipeline/device. Dropping caller
roots keeps the original pipeline/layout and original quota charges alive until
the last observation releases them. No queue or draw API is introduced here.

## WebOrdinaryPipelineOwnership/2 (c4c2b2b1)

Version2 extends the original issuance before recording: every selected stage
records the exact native module creation's WGSL byte count/SHA-256 and selected
entry. Generic graphics may select two entries in one combined module; bindless
graphics selects its two original modules. A module's bytes charge once under
the explicit source-byte budget, while stages and native modules have separate
count limits. Empty/unissued sources, missing stages, duplicate stage or a
reused module identity with different source facts fail closed. File paths,
module labels, reconstructed reflection and caller hashes are not this authority.
Source facts are recorded before native module creation and immutable after
pipeline factory issuance. The hash is a byte identity prerequisite, not a
SPIR-V/WGSL equivalence or shader access footprint proof.

Original graphics compatibility freezes color formats, depth/stencil format,
sample count, vertex stream count, topology, viewport/scissor and their enable
bits at the actual pipeline factory. Hot checks compare mutable legacy caches
to these original facts allocation-free; validation/recording consumers must use
issued facts. Independent color/stage/module/source-byte/source-CPU/snapshot-CPU
limits are mandatory. Version1 observation admission is closed; legacy pipeline
factories remain unpromoted without the original metadata quota. Binding/range
and validation callback budgets remain independent.

| Vulkan | Existing private shader/pipeline contracts unchanged; no WebGPU entry |

## WebOrdinaryShaderSizeValidation/1 (c4c2b2b2)

This private Prototype opt-in validates WebGPU's static shader binding minimum
against the original pipeline and exact cold physical groups. A zero-work
compute dispatch or render-bundle draw performs native validation without queue
submission. It does not prove arbitrary indexed shader access, SPIR-V/WGSL
equivalence, actual rendering, synchronization or completion. Generic multi-entry
graphics uses its original combined module, never a selected-entry substitute.

The cold owner retains the exact issued device and registers its loss promise
through a shared native loss-registration helper. A bounded retention pool uses
generation tickets for pending probes. Before any native creation, prepare the
result, root, callback userdata, copied vertex inputs, native-format scratch and
all owner/control blocks under the original CPU quota. Counts independently
bound pending callbacks, groups, vertex streams, native command encoders/passes/
buffers or render-bundle encoders/bundles; original group/resource quotas and
retained byte budgets remain independent. Graphics format/depth/sample/count
come only from immutable issuance. Required vertex slots must bind caller-owned
original observed VertexBuffer resources with exact offset0/explicit size;
missing, extra, wrong-device/usage or mutated resources fail before the probe.

Push separate Internal, OutOfMemory and Validation error scopes;
create/bind/dispatch(0,0,0) or draw(0,0,0,0),
finish and release all never-submitted native probe references before popping
the scopes. Every scope must deliver callback Success plus NoError before the
static-size result is authorized. Probe count and the three callbacks per probe
have independent admission limits; a validation-only scope cannot prove that
out-of-memory/internal native creation errors were absent.
Every other delivered status/type fails deterministically. Callback delivery
retires its never-submitted graph even on validation failure: native probe
references were already released, so retirement is not GPU completion proof.
Neither opaque non-null handles nor Future.id authorize success/retirement.
The callback allocates0, and stale generation tickets cannot affect later roots.

Exact-device loss fails pending results and invalidates that owner's roots.
Foreign device loss cannot close it. Callback userdata remains owned until its
real callback delivery, including late delivery after loss; no synthetic
callback, global unbounded queue or guessed timeout retires it. Results retain
their original graph and revalidate it allocation-free. Execution still reports
recording-unprepared after validation; recording and c4b2 completion contracts
are independent. Failed scopes record bounded stable diagnostic IDs, not copied
unbounded native error messages.

| Backend | Conclusion |
| --- | --- |
| Null | Equivalent metadata/status/ticket gates; DiagnosticFailure for native probe |
| Vulkan | Existing pipeline validation unchanged; WebGPU probe unavailable |
| Metal | Existing sealed function/graphics validation unchanged; WebGPU probe unavailable |
| WebGPU | Prototype native zero-work validation; explicit callback success required |

physical binding map before admitting a binary-derived range. File names,
package labels and reconstructed reflection cannot supply correlation authority.

Combined generic graphics must acquire an explicit original multi-entry module
publication/correlation contract, or continue diagnostic failure. It cannot

claim shader-language equivalence from a source hash alone. Accepted proof facts

match the original physical slot, descriptor access/visibility/type and explicit
offset/size. Unsupported instructions, missing stages/bindings, overflows and

completion remain separate planned contracts.

### ScalarComputeTargetCorrelation/1 (c4c2b2b3a)

Sealed publication/digest identity is necessary but does not by itself prove
SPIR-V/WGSL access equivalence. The first acceptance language independently
verifies the actual WGSL target's complete compute program: one writable
`array<u32>` storage declaration with explicit group/binding; exactly one
selected parameter-free compute entry and workgroup_size(1,1,1); five sequential
constant stores at indices0..4 using the same declared resource; and return.
Decimal i32/u32 constructors and a documented ASCII token/trivia profile are
explicit grammar, not guessed source matching. Extra declarations, functions,
instructions, dynamic indices, types, attributes, invalid lexemes and overflow
fail closed. Comments and whitespace cannot change token semantics. Parsing is
borrowed, bounded by input bytes, and allocates0. Reuse core ASCII whitespace
and decimal parsing; the reusable lexical cursor belongs in core, target
semantics in shader, and original native correlation in the WebGPU backend.

The existing ScalarStorageBinaryProof independently verifies SPIR-V's complete
five-word producer. Match its five literal values and20-byte interval to WGSL's
facts; match WGSL's physical slot through the publication's version5 remap and
the original issued native mapping. Match the selected Compute entry and exact
native WGSL bytes/SHA-256. This verifies this acceptance language, not general
compiler equivalence. Original native static validation remains independently
mandatory, as do original descriptor/resource observations and exact ranges.

### GraphicsShaderModuleArtifact/1 (c4c2b2b3b1)

A cold immutable YAML publication binds the five exact roles of an original
combined Slang compilation (Source, SPIR-V, MSL, WGSL, Reflection) to exactly
one selected Vertex entry and one selected Fragment entry. Each selected entry
explicitly references a separately sealed SingleEntryShaderArtifact, with its
publication identity, declared stage and entry name. Original role paths are
flat filenames; child publication paths use a bounded portable relative path
profile (nonempty flat components, '/', no '.'/'..', absolute paths or escapes).
The loader independently bounds per-file bytes, total consumed input bytes,
reference path bytes/depth, per-entry bindings and aggregate CPU metadata under
one original core quota. Both child loads debit the same total input budget;
shared source bytes read by both children are counted twice, with no heuristic
dedup or hidden budget inheritance. All failure paths clear results and release
admitted CPU ownership.

The publication requires exact byte equality between the original Source and
both selected-entry Sources, original reflection source digest and target role
filenames, exactly the two declared reflected entries with explicit stages,
GeneratedArtifact validation, and no BindlessCross authority. Child stage/name
and publication digest must match the explicit reference. Reuse the existing
strict file-role/hash/path loader and SingleEntryShaderArtifact validation;
metadata staging must derive from actual compiler outputs. Native factories
continue to freeze their actual consumed combined WGSL digest independently.

This object establishes original module byte identity and explicit per-stage

### ClosedGraphicsTriangleStorageRead/1 (c4c2b2b3b2)

Define independent complete-program acceptance profiles, not compiler-equivalence
by hash/reflection. WGSL accepts one read-only scalar uint32 runtime array with
explicit group/binding, one VertexIndex parameter, the complete nested zero/one/
otherwise triangle position selection, builtin Position output, one Fragment

an explicit uint32 literal. Every struct/member/type/reference, declaration,
operation and selected entry is consumed; extra functions/resources/operations,

and independently ensures native WGSL typing/legal identifiers. Symbol reference
and shadowing/duplicate constraints are nevertheless verified in the proof.
Core ASCII lexing gains an explicit FixedDecimalFloat profile (digits '.' digits
with optional lowercase 'f', no exponent/hex/string/suffix guessing), preserving
the original integer-only profile. Target proof accepts only the finite literal
spellings required by this version; it does not parse arbitrary float heuristics.

The independent selected Vertex SPIR-V profile proves the complete 35-instruction

binary, never assumed numeric IDs. Explicit Shader/DrawParameters capabilities,
Logical GLSL450 memory, two signed32 input variables decorated VertexIndex and
BaseVertex, a Position float4 output and one local float2 are mandatory. Verify
all declarations/types, unique definitions, selected entry/interfaces, subtraction
and bitcast/equality edges, both selection merges and branch targets, stores and
loads, the three constant float2 candidates and float4 z/w construction, return
and function end. Unknown instructions, extra functions/resources/side effects,
malformed decoration/interface/CFG/data edges fail closed. Output includes the
exact three float4 bit patterns and explicit SubtractBaseVertex input semantics.
FragmentStorageReadProof independently proves the selected complete Fragment
binary's uint32 element0 equality, literal red/green outputs and4-byte access.

Actual selected Vertex binary subtracts BaseVertex; actual WGSL vertex_index
includes firstVertex. Equivalence is therefore authorized only for direct

with TriangleList and no vertex streams. Any other draw domain fails before

fixture names. All three positions, fragment comparison literal, output interface
and byte interval must match independent proofs. Logical/physical resource slots

observations and original physical range checks remain mandatory. Combined
module publication grants provenance only and never replaces these proofs.

### WebOrdinaryComputeRecording/1 (c4c2b2b4a)

The first recording domain consumes the canonical quota-bound
ScalarComputeTargetCorrelation/1 range and its original successful native
validation/group/pipeline/resource graph. It admits exactly one direct
1x1x1 dispatch of the complete five-word Compute program. No push constants,
vertex/attachment/Graphics, transfers, readback, timestamps, staging uploads,
bindless operations or arbitrary additional commands are authorized. Other
languages require their own complete recording graph before integration.

Cold preparation independently budgets one command payload/encoder/compute
pass/finished command buffer, six error-scope callbacks (three for creation,
three for recording/finish), original group count and CPU metadata. Reuse
factory-issued native command ownership and c4b1's original completion owner/
reservation; the command retains the canonical range, while the external
recording owns the reservation to avoid a command/root/reservation cycle.
All core metadata/callback allocations and completion reservation happen before
native creation. The native command shell must not allocate a push upload ring
or infer a budget from the callback slots. Share the minimal native encoder
preparation utility with existing factories, rather than duplicate ownership
and resource cleanup logic. Preparation failures cancel never-submitted roots.

Native creation and subsequent recording/finish each push Internal,
OutOfMemory and Validation scopes with cold preallocated callback owners.
The device-global scope stack closes within each API call. All three real
Success/NoError callbacks are required for each phase. No native error handle
or non-null payload alone proves successful recording. Fixed states are
PreparationPending, Prepared, RecordingPending, Recorded, Rejected and
DeviceLost. A recorded native buffer is never submitted in this prerequisite.
The status-gated c4b2 completion path remains required before queue acceptance.

Hot recording verifies original self/range/control-block ownership, successful
preparation, exact command device/queue/recording identity, open original
completion reservation and original pipeline/group/resource/descriptor liveness
before native mutation. Draw/dispatch gates are consumed by the executor, not
only logged. Consume the already-created original physical groups and copied
dynamic offsets directly, with no CPU allocation, file IO, pipeline/group
creation or shader compilation. Bind original Compute pipeline/groups,
dispatch1, end the existing compute pass and finish at most one native command
buffer. Repeated recording and invalid dispatch fail before mutation. Generic
command entry points cannot bypass the closed recording language; direct queue
submission rejects with RHI-WEBGPU-ORDINARY-COMPLETION-UNPREPARED.

Loss/mutation/pending/native-rejected status never publishes a successful
recording. Exact-device loss invalidates the original completion reservation
and range; late native scope callbacks cannot revive the result. Dropping an
unsubmitted original recording cancels its reservation and releases its owned
command graph after any real outstanding callbacks retire. Count/CPU/native
object budgets and cancellation are independently tested. A retained graph's
ownership is evidence only, not NativeSync or shader execution evidence.

### WebGenericQueueAdmission/1 (generic completion safety gate)

The L2 invariant is that queue acceptance requires a complete original command
usage/ownership graph and an original cold completion reservation. A native
command-buffer handle, an empty command, a fence, a retained pipeline or a
resource projection alone is insufficient. Until c4b2b2b supplies the complete
graph, general WebGPU submission is DiagnosticFailure with
`RHI-WEBGPU-ORDINARY-COMPLETION-RESOURCE-GRAPH-UNPROVEN`. This applies to public
queue submission and direct generic native submission, including producer,
bindless fault and automatic readback commands. There is no default Legacy
opt-in, test-only bypass or post-submit tracker.

Admission rejects before fence reset, native queue writes/submit, callback
registration, query-state mutation, command ownership acceptance or counters.
The fence retains its original signaled/pending state, the command remains
unsubmitted, and rejection grants no completion evidence. Recovery requires
implementation of the complete original graph and the cold submission envelope;
retrying the same unprepared command cannot restore admission. No native work
means no new in-flight GPU budget, callback allocation or quarantine root.

uploads, debug markers and finish. Rejection precedes argument dereferencing,
native calls, retention, query mutation and statistics. Void texture retention
does not retain the supplied owner and latches rejection. Repeated calls cannot
restore authority. Recovery requires the full original usage and cold envelope
implementation; no runtime retry or implicit opt-in grants admission.

### CommandUsage/1 and WebGenericCommandUsage/1 (ordered metadata consumption)

The complete appended L2 vocabulary, structural scope prerequisite and exact
transition/memory/alias input boundaries are specified in
[CommandRecordingInput/1](command-recording-input-contract.md). Descriptor tables
and secondary commands have separate original owner domains and stay unproven
in the current WebGPU projection. Ownership usage validation alone does not
validate pass structure or authorize any native entry.

CommandUsage/1 defines the L2 ownership usage signature for every recording
operation. Each command names a contiguous ordered span in an original reference
projection. Empty-reference operations still occupy one command slot. Spans must
begin at the next unconsumed reference and cover the entire projection exactly;
the sole Finish is the final command and consumes no references. No skipped,
overlapping, reordered, extra or trailing reference is permitted. Draw/dispatch
signatures contain their explicit raw inputs; the executor must separately prove
their inherited pipeline/descriptor/vertex/index/pass state and scalar semantics.
Render attachments are explicit view references in color/optional-resolve order,
then optional depth/stencil. A descriptor reference is its complete original
physical binding closure, not an arbitrary raw logical set. Transfer signatures
preserve source/destination order. IndirectProducer and IndirectProcessor are
distinct original ownership domains; neither can be inferred from a BindlessRoot.
Uploads/push constants, automatic readback, query, bundle and bindless roots
require explicit original owner domains even when an API hides those owners.
Each future root observation must project its exact raw members (including
snapshot/authorization/fault lease or producer/processor scratch and access
declarations) and validate their original binding relationship. A root pointer
alone cannot establish ownership of those members. The current projection rejects
all such unproven roots. Automatic timing/resolve/readback dependencies must be
explicitly retained by their declared original usage; empty Finish/pass signatures
cannot authorize hidden query or staging creation.

| Backend | Usage metadata and native execution conclusion |
| --- | --- |
| Null | Common L2 signature/cursor validation and existing hidden-frame validator; GPU execution from this metadata is DiagnosticFailure |
| Vulkan | Existing native lowering unchanged; execution authority from this new metadata plan is DiagnosticFailure |
| Metal | Existing native lowering unchanged; execution authority from this new metadata plan is DiagnosticFailure |
| WebGPU | Explicit Prototype original cold usage metadata for projected domains; generic recording and submission remain DiagnosticFailure |

WebGenericCommandUsage/1 copies commands under an exact independent CPU quota
and explicit opt-in/version. Its command count and reference-usage count budgets
are independent of original projection/source counts, callbacks, GPU allocation
bytes and bindless limits. Preparation rejects unsupported signatures, bad spans,
missing/early/repeated Finish and leftover references before plan allocation.
Only a canonical quota-bound original projection may be retained; direct sharing
or aliased control blocks fail. Cold source mutations remain observable through
original validation. Missing source domains fail in projection preparation; a
usage budget cannot synthesize them.

Reusable core OrderedSpanCursor provides allocation-free atomic preflight and a
ticket bound to one exact cursor and position. A failed reference check consumes
none of a multi-reference span. Wrong order/kind/count/domain/raw original pointer
fails before commit; duplicate/foreign/stale tickets cannot advance a cursor.
Abort is terminal and final completeness requires all commands and references.
Cursor identity reuses core MonotonicIdSequence without allocation; address reuse
cannot revive an expired ticket, and exhaustion fails closed without recycling.
The backend metadata consumer retains the original plan while alive and checks
original ownership before preflight/commit. Separate metadata consumers are
allowed for validation; none issues native recording authority. A future native
recorder must own its original unique consumption state and cold envelope before
opening, poison it on native failure, and prevent repeat recording/submission.

automatic owners, full pass/state/transfer lowering or a submission envelope.

Backend/Platform/Production and RenderDoc remain open. Continue complete native
