### WebRecordedCommandProof/1 (original submission reuse)

L2 queue acceptance requires an original recorded command and its cold original
completion reservation. Ownership metadata, a consumed usage cursor or a native
handle does not satisfy recorded-command evidence. WebRecordedCommandProof/1 is
a backend-private abstract interface that permits the existing submission
implementation to consume future complete recorders without duplicating queue
callbacks, evidence processing or quarantine. Only explicitly approved recorder
classes may construct it. The constructor is private, copying is disabled, and
the interface is abstract; arbitrary subclasses and metadata cannot issue a
proof. Canonical quota-bound owner identity must survive a derived-to-base shared
owner conversion. Direct sharing, forged control blocks and null owners reject
before virtual evidence inspection or native work.

The proof owns the exact original command, immutable recording identity and
original reservation. Each concrete recorder must validate its original resource
graph and actual native recording scopes, and report a recorded command only
after real Success/NoError recording completion. Submission still verifies the
same command/native device/queue/reservation graph before acceptance and consumes
one exclusive preparation slot. Failed cold preparation releases its slot;
duplicate preparation or submission rejects. Completion requires actual queue
WorkDone Success and all three real submission error scopes Success/NoError.
Receipt without this evidence never publishes completion. Original command
ownership retains resources through actual success, or quarantine until exact
device loss; CPU signal semantics and host/reset generation gates are unchanged.

No new fallback, native payload, resource graph or capability is implied. The
interface introduces no independent owned allocation; its concrete object and
control block remain charged to that recorder's original metadata quota. Existing
independent reservation/submission/callback/native-resource budgets remain in
force. Submission uses the same cold callbacks and adds no post-submit allocation,
Future.id inference, native defaults, file/network IO or blocking wait.

| Backend | This proof interface and execution conclusion |
| --- | --- |
| Null | Existing validator/hidden-frame behavior; execution authority from this WebGPU proof is DiagnosticFailure |
| Vulkan | Existing native lowering unchanged; this backend-private proof grants no Vulkan work (DiagnosticFailure) |
| Metal | Existing native lowering unchanged; this backend-private proof grants no Metal work (DiagnosticFailure) |
| WebGPU | Explicit Prototype interface for existing approved original real recorders; generic/public execution remains DiagnosticFailure |

Required tests include compile-time refusal to instantiate/copy/forge the proof
from metadata, canonical derived/base owner checks, alias/direct-share rejection,
pending/unrecorded/duplicate/exhausted/quota gates, actual Compute/Graphics work,
wrapper/recorder drop, signal reset/host mutation, real error-scope failure and
exact-device loss. Quick/Null/browser paths remain required. Contract Complete /
Prototype Complete can certify this reuse prerequisite only. Complete generic
native command argument/state/usage lowering, all owner domains and public
execution/Backend/Platform/Production/RenderDoc remain open.

### FrozenTransferArguments/1 (original native-input prerequisite)

L2 transfer ownership and scalar intent are separate obligations. A prepared
transfer command must bind the exact original resource references to copied
source/destination IDs, byte offsets/count, subresource ranges, extents and row/
image pitches. FrozenTransferArguments/1 covers all four copy kinds; Finish is
the sole final zero-argument command. The snapshot is field-based, never a raw
struct-byte comparison, and contains no borrowed pointer or dynamic container.
Explicit per-mip layouts are rejected until the copy lowering implements them;
discarding a supplied layout is forbidden. This is a prerequisite of the full
generic command language, not a replacement for its remaining argument domains.

The WebGPU original snapshot consumes the existing canonical ordered usage and
original transfer resource observations. Cold preparation validates every command
against existing transfer validators, every ordered original reference and the
sole final Finish before native encoder opening. Command count and snapshot CPU
bytes have independent caller-declared budgets and an exact quota. Snapshot,
usage, owner and native command metadata/control blocks use core allocators.
Copied values outlive caller descriptors; descriptor mutation cannot rewrite
native intent. A snapshot grants no recorded-command proof or submission right.

Frozen recording preflights kind, every scalar field and exact original typed
resources before each native call, emits the copied descriptor and commits the
original usage only after successful recording. Wrong kind/values/order are
recoverable preflight rejection with no native mutation or usage consumption.
Original-owner mutation, native recording/finish failure and invalid post-native
commit poison the command permanently; restoring a source cannot revive it.
Finish rejects an unconsumed tail before native finish. Hot paths allocate zero,
perform no file/network IO or wait, and create no pipeline/shader/group/view.
Snapshots stay owned by the command until its destruction; completion may retire
the original transfer reservation earlier, but cannot release live snapshot
owners. Allocation retention and the snapshot CPU quota retire after the last
original snapshot/command owner, including wrapper drop and exact-device loss.

| Backend | Frozen copy-input conclusion |
| --- | --- |
| Null | Ownership/argument contract only; this WebGPU recorder is DiagnosticFailure |
| Vulkan | Existing copy semantics unchanged; WebGPU snapshot grants no Vulkan execution (DiagnosticFailure) |
| Metal | Existing copy semantics unchanged; WebGPU snapshot grants no Metal execution (DiagnosticFailure) |
| WebGPU | Explicit Prototype frozen input path for the existing four-copy private recorder; generic/public execution remains DiagnosticFailure |

The existing explicitly opted-in ordinary transfer prototype remains separately
identified; absence of a snapshot never chooses it from the frozen factory.
Its historical completion path does not establish generic recording error-scope
proof or the new common submission envelope. Tests require all four real native
copies/readback, exact/one-less/exhausted/count quotas, copied descriptors,
per-field/order/reference/owner/unsupported-layout failures, premature Finish,
zero native mutation/allocation on rejection, external owner drop and loss.
Contract Complete / Prototype Complete applies only to this copied transfer
input prerequisite. Full generic arguments, recording scopes, automatic owner
domains, common envelope/public capability and Backend/Platform/Production/
RenderDoc acceptance remain open.

### DepthStencilAttachmentInput/1 (complete pass-input prerequisite)

The complete generic native input plan must freeze L2 attachment intent, not
backend constants. RenderPassDepthStencilAttachmentDesc therefore explicitly
declares depthClearValue, stencilClearValue, depthReadOnly and stencilReadOnly.
Its existing load/store operations remain independent per aspect. The L2 default
clear values are depth1/stencil0 and both aspects writable; these are descriptor
values, not native defaults. Every declared depth clear value must be finite in
[0,1], and the stencil value must fit the supported eight-bit stencil formats,
even when the selected load operation does not clear. Invalid values reject
before recording or command-count mutation with
RHI-RENDER-PASS-DEPTH-CLEAR-INVALID or RHI-RENDER-PASS-STENCIL-CLEAR-INVALID.

Read-only attachment enforcement requires an original attachment/pipeline/write
binding proof. The current generic contract does not supply that proof. Either
read-only request therefore rejects on all four backends with
RHI-RENDER-PASS-DEPTH-STENCIL-READ-ONLY-UNPROVEN; a backend cannot turn it writable,
infer a suitable pipeline, or select a legacy path. This is an explicit
DiagnosticFailure conclusion for an unproven optional intent, with no capability
advertisement or opt-in. A future contract must prove pass/pipeline operations,
resource states, per-aspect native mapping, lifetime and tests before acceptance.

| Backend | Writable attachment lowering | Read-only intent |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Null | Validate complete descriptor values; no native success inferred | DiagnosticFailure |
| Vulkan | Both single- and multi-subpass VkClearValue depth/stencil fields consume the L2 values; existing writable layouts/load/store operations | DiagnosticFailure |
| Metal | Depth/stencil attachment clearDepth/clearStencil consume L2 values; load/store fields remain explicit | DiagnosticFailure |
| WebGPU | Depth/stencil clear/read-only fields and load/store operations consume the validated L2 descriptor; absent stencil explicitly disables that aspect from the resolved format | DiagnosticFailure |

WebGPU has no DontCare load action: the existing Clear refinement consumes the
same explicit L2 clear value. An absent stencil aspect is determined by the
resolved format, never guessed from backend/OS names: its native load/store are
Undefined, clear value0 and readOnly=true because it has no stencil attachment.
This is an equivalent disabled-aspect lowering, not a writable L2 stencil
request. Generic/public WebGPU recording remains closed; native diagnostic smoke
may exercise the common lowering without granting public recording authority.

The structural combined-aspect mapping does not authorize a resource format.
WebGPU exact Depth24Stencil8 resource creation remains DiagnosticFailure under
the existing format gate; no depth24plus substitution or combined-stencil native
validation is inferred from this input contract.

No resource/allocation/callback budget changes or new shader are implied.
Required evidence: common value and read-only negative tests, rejection before
state/count/native mutation on all entry paths, Null hidden-frame, both Vulkan
source paths, actual Metal/WebGPU depth clear readback from non-default values,
and RenderDoc capture or the environment's stable unavailable diagnostic. Contract
Complete is scoped to these input semantics; Backend Validated requires actual
payload/readback for each claimed backend. Platform/Production and the complete
generic native input/owner/envelope/public objective remain open.

### FrozenRenderPassArguments/1 (complete descriptor storage prerequisite)

L2 pass input storage preserves every RenderPassDesc field and ordering: both
names, every color label/target/format/load/store/resolve declaration/clear
component, the complete depth descriptor even when inactive, subpass color/input
indices and both depth flags, ordered dependencies, and layeredRendering.
Snapshots own their text and nested containers; they borrow no authoring memory.
Float comparisons use object representation, so signed zero and inactive values
are preserved without a guessed normalization. Active depth scalar/load/store
validation reuses DepthStencilAttachmentInput/1. Storage does not decide resource,
format, subpass, pipeline or state legality or issue any execution proof.

The independently versioned metadata opt-in specifies maximum color attachment,
subpass, total subpass-index, dependency and total text-byte counts, and a CPU
quota whose limit exactly matches maxCpuMetadataBytes. Count/text checks happen
before copying. The quota charges the shell, original shared control block and
every nested allocation; counts never authorize a byte increase. Allocation
failure is transactional and deterministic. Copied containers and text always
use core allocation, even if the ambient std PMR default rejects allocations.
Only immutable access and allocation-free field comparison are exposed. Any
execution must separately bind exact original owners/ordered usage, prove pass
and pipeline state, and consume the full recording batch/completion envelope.

| Backend | Input storage conclusion | Execution conclusion from storage alone |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Null | Equivalent complete field copy / validator evidence | No native execution authority |
| Vulkan | Equivalent complete field copy, no native API state inferred | DiagnosticFailure for an unproven generic batch |
| Metal | Equivalent complete field copy; existing validated public pass may consume it | No new capability or native owner authority |
| WebGPU | Equivalent complete field copy; diagnostic native helper may consume it | Generic/public recording remains DiagnosticFailure |

Stable storage diagnostics use RHI-RENDER-PASS-ARGUMENTS-{VERSION,OPT-IN,BUDGET,
QUOTA,COUNT,TEXT,ALLOCATION}. Required tests cover every mutable field, exact and
one-less count/text/CPU limits, exhausted quota, hostile default PMR, original
shared owner identity and dropped source lifetime. Actual Metal/WebGPU diagnostic
readback may show a copied value reaches a native clear; it does not validate
arbitrary pass semantics, a generic recording batch or a submission envelope.
Contract Complete / Prototype Complete only for immutable storage; full generic
recording, four-backend Backend Validated, Platform Validated and Production Ready
remain separate gates. RenderDoc unavailable diagnostic must remain visible.

### WebFrozenRenderPassBinding/1 (original ordered pass-source prerequisite)

Every BeginRenderPass in an original complete WebGenericCommandUsage/1 has one
canonical FrozenRenderPassArguments/1 shared snapshot, in command order and at
its exact command index. No extra, omitted, repeated or reordered pass record is
accepted. Attachment references resolve through the original Resource projection,
in color/optional resolve order then optional depth order. Each typed view has
the exact captured ID, view payload, parent and allocation owner, explicit
RenderAttachment usage, aspect, and matching declared format (Unknown preserves
the existing resolved-format contract). Color intent requires a non-depth format;
depth intent requires the resolved depth format. Snapshot or projection alias
control blocks cannot impersonate the originals. Stored snapshots and the whole
original usage graph keep their own independently charged quotas/owners.

The separate record-count/CPU quota bounds only this binding storage. It cannot
derive resource, snapshot-text, native-view or callback capacities. Validation
and comparison allocate zero and do not inspect mutable authoring memory after
preparation. A raw BeginRenderPass comparison must supply the same complete
descriptor and original view pointers; success only permits the future full
batch executor to check its original ordered usage ticket. Storage issues no
ticket, encoder, command proof, reservation, completion envelope or capability.
Any invalid source becomes a deterministic rejection before native opening.

Null remains a reference validator; Vulkan/Metal retain their existing native
owner/state gates and gain no authority from this WebGPU-private metadata.
WebGPU generic/public execution remains DiagnosticFailure until complete native
state, one synchronous recording/error-scope batch and the original submission
envelope are proven. Stable binding diagnostics use
RHI-WEBGPU-PASS-ARGUMENTS-{VERSION,OPT-IN,BUDGET,QUOTA,ORIGINAL-USAGE,CAPACITY,
ORDER,ORIGINAL-SNAPSHOT,REFERENCE,VIEW,INPUT,ALLOCATION}. Pure/native-source tests,
Null hidden-frame and actual browser lifetime/mutation/device-loss evidence are
mandatory. This binding prerequisite cannot complete the full generic objective
or grant Backend/Platform/Production/RenderDoc promotion.

### WebPublicTransitionAdmission/1 (pre-mutation safety gate)

Public WebGPU transition must consume the same admission conclusion as the raw
barrier entry before calling transitionWebGpuRhiBuffer/Texture. A generic batch
without proof fails with RHI-WEBGPU-COMMAND-RECORDING-USAGE-UNPROVEN; an ordinary
prepared language requires its owner and an ordinary transfer language rejects
unsupported barriers. Neither rejection may acquire the core mutation journal,
change whole/subresource state, increment recorded/native counts, or invoke native
work. LastValidation and the returned complete requested barrier metadata expose
the rejection. A missing native payload fails with the distinct stable
RHI-WEBGPU-BARRIER-NATIVE-PAYLOAD-MISSING and updates the same complete result
and LastValidation, without pretending it evaluated a nonexistent native graph.
Keeping the rejected encoder alive cannot block a different
authorized metadata journal. Native admission failure after future admitted state
mutation still needs the full terminal batch protocol; this gate provides no
rollback guess, barrier execution, Sync capability or public promotion.

Null's existing validator transaction and Vulkan/Metal's existing payload and
semantic gates remain their respective conclusions; no new lowering or execution
authority is derived for them. WebGPU remains DiagnosticFailure before mutation.
Contract Complete / Prototype Complete evidence requires fast public state/count/
journal negatives, Null hidden-frame and actual browser native-call instrumentation.
Full native barrier arguments/state/lowering and synchronous command batch remain
open, independently of this safety gate and RenderDoc unavailable diagnostic.

### WebPublicPassAdmission/1 (pre-mutation pass gate)

Public render/compute/transfer pass entry must reject its unproven recording
language before attachment scratch allocation or retention, active-pass/count
mutation, or pass-wrapper creation. Render attachment scalar/load/store diagnostics
remain the first input check; that check grants no source authority. A missing
native payload rejects with RHI-WEBGPU-PASS-NATIVE-PAYLOAD-MISSING. Render/Compute
consume the same non-transfer raw gate; Transfer consumes its existing prepared
recording gate, which only permits the separately opted-in private transfer
prototype. An unprepared public generic transfer encoder cannot create a
stat-only pass wrapper and increment recorded count. Keeping the denied encoder
alive must preserve the same count/state and subsequent deterministic rejection.

This is a WebGPU DiagnosticFailure admission repair. Null/Vulkan/Metal preserve
their existing validator/native payload gates; no new backend semantics, lowering
payload, capability or completion authority is inferred. Quick/Null and actual
browser no-native-call negative evidence must precede Contract Complete /
Prototype Complete for the safety gate; arbitrary native passes/full batches,
Backend/Platform/Production and RenderDoc remain independently unproven.

### WebGenericCommandProjection/1 (original ownership closure prerequisite)

L2 command ownership requires original resource/allocation owners, pipeline
identity and descriptor revision/lifetime/binding identity. Copying native
handles or retaining a command shell cannot satisfy this invariant. This
backend-private cold projection composes existing original transfer/resource
projections and canonical quota-bound pipeline/physical-group observations. It
copies the declared reference list; each covered reference resolves to one
exact original payload object in a declared source family. Reference order is
preserved as metadata. It does not yet prove that every actual command consumed
that list, validate draw/transfer semantics or install a completion envelope.

The independent budget declares transfer/resource source counts, pipeline and
physical-group counts, reference count, each reference-domain count, allocation
projection occurrence count, unique retained allocation bytes and exact graph
CPU quota. Source projections retain their own independent original quotas and
native object budgets; the graph cannot infer them from callbacks or bindless
limits. Graph vectors use core BoundedSequence and core PMR allocation. Source
arrays are borrowed only during preparation; all accepted shared observations
and copied references are owned afterward. Transfer/resource roots use the same canonical quota-bound shareOriginal
issuance as pipeline/group observations. Direct sharing and forged control
blocks grant no admission. Shared roots keep their original
CPU quotas through graph retirement; early rejection adds no new root.

Buffer/texture transfer references consume original transfer observations;
ordinary buffer/view/sampler references consume original resource observations;
pipeline references consume issued original pipeline observations; descriptor
references consume the complete original physical-group closure. The latter
includes its original pipeline, descriptor revisions, offsets and resources.
All source families must name the same native device. Null, aliased or unissued
pipeline/group owners, stale source identity or uncovered references reject.
BindlessRoot/Upload/Readback/Query/RenderBundle references explicitly return
`RHI-WEBGPU-COMMAND-PROJECTION-DOMAIN-UNPROVEN` before graph allocation. Nonzero
domain budgets cannot turn absent original ownership into success.

Reusable core SharedAllocationProjection computes unique bytes directly from
the immutable original source mapping without a temporary materialized ledger.
It counts every projected occurrence against its own count budget. Repeated
tokens require the same original control block, backing owner and exact byte
charge. Distinct backing under one charge, forged aliases, missing owners,
overflow and one-less byte capacity fail closed. Empty entries represent an
object such as a sampler with no separately registered resource allocation;
its original native-object and metadata budgets remain enforced by its source.
Hot validation/reference lookup allocates0 and issues no native API calls.

| Backend | Ownership projection and execution conclusion |
| --- | --- |
| Null | Common core accounting/negative validation only; no native proof |
| Vulkan | Existing original retention lowering unchanged; this WebGPU projection grants no Vulkan execution |
| Metal | Existing original retention lowering unchanged; this WebGPU projection grants no Metal execution |
| WebGPU | Explicit Prototype cold ownership closure for covered domains; generic execution remains DiagnosticFailure |

Even an empty or valid projection returns
`RHI-WEBGPU-ORDINARY-COMPLETION-RESOURCE-GRAPH-UNPROVEN` from its execution gate.
Completing c4b2b2b still requires every actual generic recording reference to

an original cold reservation/evidence/loss envelope and public capability gates.
This prerequisite cannot close c4b2b2b, c4b2b2, c4b2b, c4b2 or the bindless plan.

RenderDoc remains RHI-RENDERDOC-TOOL-UNAVAILABLE; no visible-output, Backend,
Platform, Production or public execution promotion follows.

### WebOrdinaryCompletionSignal/1 (generic completion publication prerequisite)

Define the optional original CPU completion signal separately from recording,
submission callbacks and retained GPU resources. Its cold factory requires
version1, explicit Prototype opt-in, an independent nonzero state count and
CPU quota. Canonical quota-bound ownership is mandatory; a legacy facade state,
stack object or forged shared control block cannot substitute. No NativeSync
authority follows from a CPU signal.
Each factory transaction produces exactly one state within its declared count;
the shared quota bounds aggregate bytes. Original graph/pool counts remain
separate. A private issuance flag also rejects direct core sharing that lacks
the version/opt-in factory contract, even if its control block and quota match.

Reuse core MonotonicIdSequence in an allocation-free CompletionSignal domain:
one non-recycling generation, one exclusive reservation across all graph owners,
and a completed generation. Reset and explicit host signal advance the generation
and invalidate prior receipts; generation exhaustion fails closed. A reset does
not release an outstanding reservation. Original graph retirement, cancellation
or exact-device invalidation releases that exact reservation. Stale callbacks
cannot publish into a newer generation or overwrite its completion evidence.
Owner mutation and native callback delivery are externally serialized, as are
the existing completion pools; polling remains atomic and never blocks.

The original cold completion graph captures this signal reservation before
native encoder work. Submit validates it without resetting or reconstructing it.
Only real WorkDone Success plus all three actual Success/NoError submission
scopes may complete that original generation, before retiring its original graph.
Queue Success alone remains Pending. Scope/queue failure, generation mutation
or exact-device loss never publishes success. The same failed graph/signal claim
stays quarantined until exact-device loss; other owners cannot reuse its signal.
Unsubmitted cancellation permits a fresh original reservation.

| --- | --- |
| Null | Generation/reservation/evidence validator only |
| Vulkan | Existing native fence/completion lowering unchanged |
| Metal | Existing native callback/completion lowering unchanged |
| WebGPU | Private Prototype CPU signal on original cold Compute/Graphics graphs; generic resource admission remains unproven |

acceptance and across held real promises, external owner drop, exact-device loss
and late callbacks. The active generic tracker is not authorized by this
prerequisite; c4b2b2 still requires its full original resource graph replacement.

## CompletionRetentionPool/2 atomic submission admission prerequisite

Status: Contract Complete / Prototype Complete for CPU storage. This is reusable
CPU lifetime storage and does not provide native submission or completion proof.

Single owner thread and external serialization remain mandatory. submitWith takes
an exact live reservation ticket and a noexcept bool callback on that original
stored value. Invalid/stale/duplicate/submitted/closed/failed tickets reject before
invoking the callback. Once those checks pass, the callback runs exactly once;
false leaves the reservation unsubmitted and cancellable, true marks it submitted
without another fallible admission step. A callback must not reenter/mutate the
pool or destroy its owner; it must leave its own transaction unchanged on false,
or permanently invalidate its partial changes before false. Callback validation
and state publication are one externally serialized CPU operation. This creates
no GPU order, successful submission, completion, fence or timeline authority.

Existing submit remains a no-op-commit convenience using the same admission path.
The pool and callback allocate0 after cold capacity allocation. All stored owner
budgets remain explicit and independent of this operation. An unproven native
result still quarantines retained owners until a separately proven completion or
terminal invalidation. Native API calls occur only after accepted admission and
must preserve their preexisting failure/quarantine contracts.

Null/Vulkan/Metal/WebGPU share this CPU prerequisite. All4 native dependency
execution outcomes remain DiagnosticFailure for OriginalDependencyStateTransaction/1
until a separate approved lowering and backend-private execution consume it.
Fast unit tests cover stale/duplicate/closed/failed tickets, rejected callbacks,
retry, exactly-once publication and retained owner lifetime; Null hidden-frame and
both browser feature modes remain smoke paths. No Backend/Platform/Production
promotion follows a CPU admission test.

pass. Same core journal/value fixture executes in unit, Null bindless contract
and both real browser modes, proving exact-ticket-before-callback, rejected

once, submitted unproven owner quarantine and terminal invalidation allocation0.

nativeErrors=[]. Evidence original-completion-admission-* YAML/log/XML. Native

and layout remain immutable; duplicate aliases/foreign owners and already recorded

completion. Cancellation before mapping retires its slot and permits a fresh cold
reservation. Once a native map is issued the source cannot be republished through
another sink. Its original callbacks retain it through actual callback retirement.

Native start consumes the sink reservation only after original queue and scope
completion prove copy readiness. Raw mapping cannot bypass an exclusive sink ticket.
All slot/output positions already exist; no callback creates or grows a queue. The
owner thread polls a bounded original entry scan, preserving exact pool tickets.
Only the original map's Published state plus original sealed-result validation yields
a Completed CPU snapshot. Partial map/scope evidence remains unavailable. Native
Rejected publication waits for every original map/scope callback to be observed;
actual loss fails deterministically, while already Published immutable snapshots
remain valid after loss. Cancellation after native issue suppresses delivery while
retaining the original entry until terminal drain, or sink destruction; native
callbacks retain outstanding resources if callers drop the sink. An occupied
output rejects without discarding the caller's previous result. Delivery retains
the original input/label/layout/storage and never exposes native handles.

Independent sink/record CPU quotas and explicit pending/map callback/mip/retained
native/content/result/label bounds pay every live reservation. Retained source byte
budgets conservatively count each original graph, without guessed resident bytes.
Admission preflights capacity before source reservation/native mutation; exhausted
publication positions fail before recording. Hot native-start/drain/cancel operations
perform no allocation, file IO or blocking wait. Destruction releases original
source ownership; late native callbacks remain owner-backed and cannot use a dead
sink because callbacks do not access sink storage in this prerequisite.

## TextureReadbackContentIntent/1

L2 content semantics are independent of object lifetime and completed-upload
provenance. `Unspecified` fails closed. `QueueOrderedSnapshot` requires version1
and explicit Prototype opt-in: copy the selected physical subresources as they
exist when the admitted texture-to-buffer command executes in the source device's
queue order. Earlier queue writes precede this copy; concurrent unordered producers
are unsupported and must be rejected by the original state transaction. The source
lease transfers the exact exclusive C++ texture object. It neither freezes contents
at lease creation nor proves initialization, authored bytes or upload completion.
No replacement pixels, CPU replay or synthesized upload declaration is permitted.
Only the original native copy/submission/map/error-scope join can publish bytes.

The existing completed-original-upload profile retains its original completed graph,
coverage checks, exclusive content policy and byte accounting without alteration.
Snapshot intent cannot select that profile or use an exclusive-upload texture.
Delivered original results expose the selected content mode. Snapshot sources retain
zero authored CPU content bytes; this is a measured absence of an upload graph,
not zero-filled output. Native allocation, padded result and staging charges remain.

| Backend | Version1 lowering | Execution gate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Null | Validator only | DiagnosticFailure: RHI-READBACK-CONTENT-NULL-VALIDATOR-ONLY |
| Vulkan | DiagnosticFailure | RHI-READBACK-CONTENT-BACKEND-UNIMPLEMENTED |
| Metal | DiagnosticFailure | RHI-READBACK-CONTENT-BACKEND-UNIMPLEMENTED |
| WebGPU | Explicit Prototype native queue-ordered texture copy | Requires exact public lease, live registry/state transaction, destination creation, recorded command, queue completion, actual map and all scopes |

Budgets and ownership use OriginalReadbackColdBudget/1's four exact quota domains,
OriginalReadbackDeviceStagingBudget/1 and the existing publication retention pool.
No additional proof issuer, public/general execution capability, NativeSync or
legacy global-queue replacement follows from this private content profile.
Cancellation suppresses publication but cannot retire any issued callback ownership;
pre-map device pending accounting remains a separate integration requirement.
Native creation failure is terminal, retry requires a new explicit request after
all callbacks retire. Device loss rejects new work and releases retained native
allocation charges only with the last original owner. No blocking/hot allocation,
file IO, native defaults or shader compilation is introduced.

## OwnedTextureReadbackPreparation/1 (Contract Complete; CPU Prototype Complete)

The distinct `arp.rhi.TextureReadbackOwnedPreparation/1` interface consumes an
original public plan, quota-bound request/layout/input/label/statistics owners,
owned GpuTexture reference and explicit versioned Prototype snapshot/barrier/label
intent. It never accepts a borrowed source in place of owned lifetime. Every
pre-admission error preserves the caller's UniquePtr. Successful cold admission
must allocate the opaque preparation and all source/destination admission metadata,
validate the actual device registry/native source, reserve original pending/native/
result budgets, copy the pass label and then atomically transfer that exact source.
Native creation is a separate hot action after admission. No facade/Null receipt
proves native execution. Ordinary/default/completed-upload content is rejected;
a verified completed-upload public profile requires its own future contract.

The public preparation exposes only exact L2 request, phase and diagnostic.
CreationPrepared -> InitializeCreation -> CreationPending -> CreationReady is
followed by explicit PrepareRecordingCold -> RecordingPrepared -> Record ->
RecordingPending -> RecordingReady, PrepareSubmissionCold -> SubmissionPrepared ->
Submit -> SubmissionPending -> SubmissionCompleted, StartMapping -> MappingPending
-> Published/Rejected/DeviceLost. Cold advancement may allocate through the exact
Request quota. Phase, hot actions, cancel, poll and shutdown must not allocate,
block, access files or issue shader/pipeline compilation. The action validator
has no state mutation; actual original scope/queue/map owners alone expose readiness.
All terminal/pending/unknown phases reject the six execution/preparation actions.

A published delivery retains exact original request plus a quota-bound immutable
CPU snapshot exposing only original L2 request/statistics, content mode, padded
bytes/capacity and canonical TextureReadbackViewAnalysis values. It must prove
actual original submission/map/full callbacks/numerical completion before publishing.
Failure/loss echoes exact request and stable diagnostic with no snapshot, bytes or
numerical success. Busy output is preserved. Cancel/drop suppresses delivery while
retaining the exact original pending slot and native/CPU owners until all issued
callbacks drain. Consumer-held output keeps its original quota/lifetime charges.
No upper layer observes/caches native resources, allocation objects or API parameters.

Shutdown closes admission, cancels original requests and consumes actual runtime
facts. Outstanding callbacks require DrainIssuedCallbacks even after device loss.
A drained unproven submitted graph requires AwaitNativeLoss and keeps quarantine;
callback receipt cannot clear it. Remaining publication requests require
DrainUnretiredRequests. Only an empty/drained, non-quarantined (or actually lost)
context can detach its original device-loss registration. Detachment must suppress
late native loss delivery before userdata retirement. Device/request owners dropped
by the caller cannot discard issued callback or quarantine lifetime.

Version1 four-backend lowering: Null ValidatorOnly (execution unavailable), Vulkan
DiagnosticFailure RHI-READBACK-OWNED-VULKAN-UNIMPLEMENTED, Metal DiagnosticFailure
RHI-READBACK-OWNED-METAL-UNIMPLEMENTED, WebGPU PrototypeRequiresDeviceContext with
execution unavailable/RHI-READBACK-OWNED-DEVICE-CONTEXT-UNIMPLEMENTED until actual
device context and original shutdown registration are integrated. Unknown backend
rejects deterministically. ExtensionOptIn is independent of baseline/tracked/general
capabilities and NativeSync; no applyTo/default descriptor enables it. Default
configure/prepare/poll/shutdown all fail closed, preserve source/output and create
no dummy payload. Public API is declared now; GpuDevice exposure/native integration
remains disabled. Legacy borrowed/global poll remains until delivery equivalence.

## OriginalDeviceLossRegistration/1 (Contract Complete; scoped Backend Validated)

The owned readback cold plan now requires an explicit version1 Prototype loss
registration descriptor: registration count, callback count and exact Device
CPU metadata quota. The original context consumes one registration/one callback;
these ceilings never imply additional automatic subscriptions or NativeSync.
Null is ValidatorOnly, Vulkan/Metal are deterministic Unimplemented, WebGPU is
an explicit Native Prototype with real device.lost binding required. Missing
counts, foreign descriptor/plan/quota or exhausted metadata fail before binding.

A separate original completion factory preserves the existing legacy strong
observer path. Its Device-charged registration retains the exact original owner
until actual loss or explicit idle/completed shutdown. Pending reserved recording
or submitted graphs reject detach; failed submitted graphs keep quarantine until
actual native loss. Detach closes further graph admission. Public preparation
shutdown must additionally drain all issued creation/recording/submission/map
callbacks and retire publication requests before invoking owner detachment.

The native promise owns one bounded state with callback/userdata and one-shot
phase. Before detach succeeds, the native state clears userdata/function and
suppresses later delivery. Before actual loss callback delivery, it clears them
and enters Delivered. Only then may C++ retire its envelope/strong ownership.
An externally serialized core callback gate prevents duplicate/reentrant delivery,
detach during delivery and rearm; it proves lifetime only. No weak/raw-pointer
substitution, growing global registration map or completion success is allowed.
Actual loss invalidates the exact pool/quarantine, without reviving GPU success.
Caller drop retains subscription/graphs until loss; idle detachment is explicit.

The plan owns reusable core count ledgers and one monotonic loss binding gate.
Any native binding attempt consumes that plan's binding once; C++ allocation
failure before the attempt permits retry, native binding failure requires a
fresh explicitly authored plan. Successful detach releases C++ count/metadata
charges but never rearms that plan. This prevents recycling an authored count
ceiling to accumulate unbounded detached native promise closures. Each detached
native closure may survive until real loss but holds no C++ userdata. The WebGPU
link contract explicitly includes Emscripten's supported val ownership runtime.

## OwnedReadbackDeviceBudgetContext/1 (Contract Complete; scoped Backend Validated)

This prerequisite attaches the distinct owned preparation interface to actual
WebGPU GpuDevice. One immutable original plan supplies all Device/Request/Resource/
Result quotas and mapped descriptors. The context reuses the actual device's
resource registry and composes aggregate staging, publication and original
completion owners. All fallible context/staging/sink allocation precedes native
loss binding. Failure publishes no context and consumes no caller source. An
active context rejects reconfiguration; a consumed native binding plan cannot be
recycled after shutdown. Device metadata remains bounded by the authored quota.

Public executionAvailable stays false. Public admission and poll deterministically
return RHI-READBACK-OWNED-ADMISSION-UNIMPLEMENTED while configured, or
RHI-READBACK-OWNED-CONTEXT-UNCONFIGURED otherwise; source/output are preserved.
Atomic owned-source adoption and execution advancement remain the next substage.
Idle shutdown requires no publication requests, retained staging or completion
graphs, detaches the original subscription before freeing context, and allocates
zero. Actual loss consumes the original subscription; subsequent idle shutdown
retires metadata without attempting to rearm/detach delivered callbacks. Device
destruction uses the same idle shutdown. Admission remains closed, so this stage
cannot introduce issued public requests; future admission must implement callback
and quarantine drain before widening destruction semantics.

Four-backend conclusion: Null ValidatorOnly, Vulkan/Metal deterministic unavailable
via the L2 owned preparation contract, WebGPU native Prototype budget context
only with public execution unavailable. Existing legacy borrowed/global poll,
general/tracked/NativeSync and all baseline capability flags remain unchanged.
Context composition and actual loss lifecycle require real browser evidence;
CPU/Null validation alone does not promote Backend/Platform/Production status.

The actual GpuDevice context also has a monotonic binding-consumed gate. One
native binding attempt consumes this device's context lifetime, including failed
binding after native subscription. Shutdown never rearms it, even with a newly
authored plan; RHI-READBACK-OWNED-CONTEXT-CONSUMED rejects reconfiguration. This
prevents an application from accumulating detached promises by replacing plans
on the same device. C++ metadata failure before binding preserves retry. A new
context requires a new explicitly budgeted GpuDevice instance.

## OriginalReadbackColdSourceAdoption/1

Atomic owned-source admission uses delayed adoption. An exact quota-bound L2
lease receipt retains immutable request metadata and the candidate address only.
It owns no resource before commit; ordinary validation and sourceTexture fail
closed. Cold validation requires the caller's still-owned exact candidate and
rechecks request, source id and descriptor layout. Stored addresses are never
dereferenced; abandoning the receipt after caller destruction is safe.

All downstream cold metadata, budget charges, native CPU envelope and callback
retention must be prepared synchronously before commit. Cold-only validators
require the exact live candidate argument; they cannot grant readiness, create
a GPU object, issue callbacks or change ordinary validators. Final adopt validates
original shared identity, exact candidate and current descriptor before a one-shot
allocation-free UniquePtr move. Failed adoption preserves the caller. No rollback
reconstruction, raw release/re-adoption or fallback is involved. The old immediate
lease factory composes this cold receipt and final adoption.

## OriginalReadbackOwnedAdvancement/1

The opaque owned preparation advances explicitly. CreationReady permits one cold
recording composition using the exact immutable plan, retained destination, copied
UTF-8 pass label, frozen source barrier, original completion owner and statistics
plan. A quota-bound immutable cold receipt retains the original graph, recording,

Hot record consumes the bound original receipt and only actual three-scope
recording completion exposes RecordingReady. A second explicit cold call creates
original submission/callback metadata and binds its drain retention under Request
quota. Failure preserves RecordingReady and allows retry. Hot submit consumes that
exact submission; actual queue completion plus all scopes are required for
SubmissionCompleted. Hot map consumes the preallocated original mapping/publication
ticket; map/read/unmap/scopes and original numerical callback determine publication.
No hot path may compile shaders, allocate C++ envelopes, perform IO or manufacture
proof. Wrong-phase actions deterministically fail without advancing or allocating.

Cancellation stops further actions, cancels original publication and retains
issued callback owners. CanceledDraining lasts through creation/recording/submission/
map callback debt, and through an unproven submitted graph until actual native
loss. Retired means this request has no outstanding callbacks or quarantine; it
does not assert native completion. Native rejection/loss phases come from original
owners, and Published requires original map plus numerical success. Existing
recording/submission remain the sole native proof issuers.

## BorrowedTextureReadbackRetirement/1

The borrowed legacy ABI does not declare the original request/layout/statistics,
owned source lease, CPU domains, callback count or per-device result namespace.

therefore deterministically fails before public encoder/resource inspection or GPU work.

before its retirement decision; none of these paths issue native work.
The extension remains discoverable: schedule returns a stable retired diagnostic;
poll returns false without altering caller output. Budget/tracked opt-ins remain
unsupported on these retired adapters and never dispatch into owned preparation.

| Metal | ExplicitBoundedLegacy only after its original configureBudget; existing native completion/device-owned queue contract and tests remain required |
| WebGPU | RetiredDiagnosticFailure, RHI-READBACK-BORROWED-WEBGPU-RETIRED |

texture map queue are removed. Original map/scope callback tests remain in the
bounded publication path. Vulkan's native/recorder entry points also reject before

implementation is removed; no public or command execution admission is granted.

preserves its bounded Legacy contract without promoting owned or base capability.

## OwnedReadbackResolvedContextLowering/1

Static `TextureReadbackOwnedPreparationContract::lowering` reports an unconfigured
extension. `resolveLowering` keeps Null/Vulkan/Metal and unknown backend conclusions
closed regardless of context facts. Only WebGPU's original configured native
payload, armed loss registration, accepting bounded pool and absence of actual loss
resolve to `PrototypeContextReady`. Configuration requires the original versioned
plan's explicit Prototype opt-in. This is extension admission metadata, never
native recording/completion proof or a base capability.

The public L2 `executionDecision` exposes the resolved disposition, version,
availability and stable diagnostic. `executionAvailable` derives from that same
decision; backend preparation consumes its availability and diagnostic before
source inspection/adoption. Idle shutdown, draining closure and native loss close
admission immediately. Poll still drains originally issued work after closure and
never treats admission availability as completion evidence. Generic/tracked,
bindless, NativeSync and completed-upload content profiles remain independent.

## OwnedReadbackConcurrentPublicContext/1

One original context admits at most its explicit pending count and aggregate
staging/result/native/content/label ceilings. Identity uses original requestId,
never label matching. Poll may deliver a completed second request while the first
has not started mapping, or while its canceled creation callbacks remain pending.
Delivery frees its pending slot; the original native graph still charges staging
until its final owner releases. Detached CPU output retains its independent
Request/Result charges. Failed duplicate/count/aggregate admission preserves the
original candidate and rolls back every cold charge; capacity can be retried only
after the corresponding native owner retires. Per-request cancel never cancels a
peer's original ticket or forwards canceled output. Existing four-backend owned
Prototype/retired-borrowed decisions remain unchanged.

duplicate/count/retained-staging rejection and retry; canceled original creation
partial receipts coexist with peer success, full drain emits no canceled output.

## OwnedReadbackPublicDeviceIsolation/1

Logical contexts own their original registry, immutable plan and CPU domains.
Sharing a physical native device does not share original resource admission or
pending request namespaces. Receiver-authored input/layout/request/statistics with
the correct Request quota still cannot adopt a foreign logical device's texture;
exact registry observation must reject before native buffer creation, preserving
candidate ownership and all quota/count charges. A valid source with a foreign
Request domain independently rejects. Identical caller requestId/label values in
two original contexts deliver only their own exact Request/snapshot owners and GPU
bytes; draining or shutting down one context cannot retire a peer's ticket, native
allocation or quota. This extends public isolation evidence, with no change to
four-backend lowering or the selected Prototype scope.

## ColdOwnedObjectSlot/1 generic source lifetime prerequisite (Contract Complete)

The core slot extracts readback's existing two-stage ownership transfer. A cold
bind remembers one caller object address without dereferencing it after caller
death. It exposes no owned object before acceptance. Hot adoption requires the
still-owned exact candidate plus a noexcept caller validator that rechecks stable
identity and semantic binding; address equality cannot establish identity or
exclude allocator address reuse. The validator must allocate0 and perform no IO
or GPU work. Failure preserves caller ownership; accepted adoption moves the
original core UniquePtr once, preserving its original derived deleter and quota.
Receipt abandonment retains no object. The slot cannot be copied/moved/rebound.

TextureReadbackSourceLease uses this shared mechanism while retaining its exact
request/layout/source-id checks and original quota-bound shared-owner gate. Four
backend conclusions, GPU staging/recording/completion and public Prototype policy
are unchanged. This is a lifetime primitive, never GPU/native execution proof.
A full generic source bridge still requires L2 source domain, complete command/
input/state/completion ownership and four-backend lowering before integration.

actual browser reports clean. Exact original object/deleter/quota, failure ownership,
caller death and exhausted hot allocation0 pass. Evidence `generic-source-cold-slot-*`
YAML/log/XML. Core ownership prerequisite only; no generic execution promotion.

## Original stream compilation and selected projection traversal (2026-10-04)

`OriginalCommandStreamCompilation/1` compiles the complete supported immutable
`OriginalCommandInputs` profile into pointer-free ordered operations. Each operation
binds its original command, sparse descriptor, reference span, and family-specific
expansion ordinal. Scalar/dependency operations have no expansion; copy elements,
buffer uploads, texture upload/readback mips and attachment references use separate
ordinal spaces. Explicit descriptors and existing `TransferCopyProjection` and
upload/readback layout contracts determine counts. No extent, pitch, staging content,
native resource generation or completion identity is guessed.

Independent opted-in limits cover commands, descriptors, references, copy elements,
buffer uploads, upload/readback mips, render passes, attachment references, buffer
upload **declared** bytes, texture upload CPU pixel bytes, readback **declared** bytes,
CPU closure owners and metadata. Repeated commands charge repeated work/declared
bytes even when the same original snapshot is retained once. Source and Graph quota
aliases are rejected; materialized program metadata uses an explicitly bounded
Recording quota within the original Source's authored Recording ceiling. CPU owners
are the original Inputs closure plus the program, not native registration owners.

Existing input/order/provenance validation runs first. `PreflightReplay` validates
all operations/counts before cold allocation or the first CPU projection visitor.
The immutable program retains the exact original Inputs; it does not recreate the
root, state graph or journal. `OriginalCommandAccessValidation`, the original state
plan, native closure, issuer, scope/queue/loss bindings and independent Source,
Recording/Submission/Callback/Result/staging budgets remain separate prerequisites.
Zero disables an absent family. A budget failure publishes no partial program and
invokes no projection visitor. Visitor failure stops subsequent consumption and
invalidates the whole cold projection; partially built native work is never submitted.

`OriginalStreamSelectedProjectionTraversal/1` permits the existing private selected
CopyPass lowering to consume this allocation-free operation traversal and exact
sparse descriptor indices. Source-authored command/descriptor/reference limits,
selected admission's render-pass limit and independently authored native copy limit

copy expansion must agree with the operation's first ordinal/count. This traversal
owns no additional retained program or callback and cannot widen the exact selected
language or either proof issuer. It creates no GPU work.

Canonical map receipt adapter contract /1 (selected adapter verified 2026-10-05):
L2 map intent remains exact original Request -> one MapRead over offset0 and its
padded byteCapacity; no map outcome may grant Submission or snapshot authority.
Null validates intent only; Vulkan/Metal diagnose absent receipt payload; selected
private WebGPU /5 may cold-bind C-map userdata and the existing three native
scope receipt adapters to the exact Result Request and original staging buffer.
Canonical execution/publication remain closed until same-State/Graph retirement
is implemented. Earlier /1-/4 profiles allocate no map receipts.

The backend-private native C adapter must consume actual wgpuBufferMapAsync,
wgpuBufferReadMappedRange and wgpuBufferUnmap algorithms, requiring emdawn and
GPUBuffer.mapState agreement at each access boundary. Map ranges use spec-defined
four-byte size alignment, offset0 and explicit native MapRead usage. All adapter
objects/userdata live in cold Callback-owned original slots; no hot core-owned
allocation or new RecordedCommand proof issuer is allowed. Native scope listeners
are the existing cold NativeCompletionReceipt/1 payloads. Caller/enum/boolean
cannot synthesize delivery. C-map callback reports only native map status.

Guarded native invocation must catch JS throws across a trivial C thunk. Unknown
map/read/unmap invocation fails with stable diagnostics, preserves the exact cold
slot/root/Graph, and never claims callback/lifetime completion. C callbacks may
arrive synchronously when wgpuBufferUnmap aborts a pending emdawn future; caller
must retain the original state through the entire call. Only actual callback plus
bridge/native unmap agreement, or a proven native loss teardown with all future
userdata drained, permits release. Listener suppression alone is insufficient.
Do not manually alter emdawn state/future tables. External emdawn event allocation
is opaque and hot in this library version: selected use remains explicit
Prototype; core hot-allocation0 is not an assertion about bridge/driver allocation,
and cannot promote this path to Production Ready.

Canonical ReadbackMapDrain/1 contract (pure contract verified 2026-10-05): native
map retirement is separate from independent Result publication. Facts describe
original /5 expected maps, actually invoked C maps, genuine map callbacks,
copied-and-unmapped maps, map scopes drained/successful, original foreign receipt
drain and genuine queue/loss evidence. These are validation inputs only; no public
facts/enum/bool may create native authority or retire the original graph.

Each issued C map must deliver its actual callback and prove bridge/native unmap
agreement before its raw userdata or original graph can retire. Every issued map
owns exactly three original scope receipt slots. Suppressed scope listeners may
count only toward foreign userdata drain, never scope success; suppressing map
listeners is not supported by the C adapter. Invocation uncertainty, cancel,
native map/scope errors and physical loss invalidate-and-retain until original
callbacks, every issued C map and safe unmap have drained. Queue suppression is
not GPU completion: MaySubmit requires genuine queue receipt or actual loss even
when all userdata is suppressed. Inconsistent/overflow facts fail closed.

Successful drain requires genuine same-root Submission proof, all expected maps
issued, exact copies/unmaps, all scopes successful and all foreign receipts
drained. It permits native graph retirement with Result still unpublished, never
snapshot publication. Null validates these rules; Vulkan/Metal/WebGPU canonical
execution remain deterministic DiagnosticFailure until their private native
runtime consumes this contract. Existing /4 success retirement must remain
unchanged; /5 must keep the existing State/Graph/loss/seal across mapping and use
Recording/Submission as the only RecordedCommand proof issuers.

CanonicalSnapshotPublication /1 (selected /5 runtime integrated):
Publication requires a separate explicit versioned Prototype intent, consumed

The existing original Submission issuer must prevalidate the genuine same-State
MapDrain RetireUnpublished result, exact original Request/layout and independent
Result-quota binding, complete once-only unpublished snapshot coverage and absence

journal are mandatory before freezing all independent snapshots. Freeze is a
bounded allocation-free CPU operation with no native calls or callback reentry;
prevalidate the complete set before changing any publication flag. Any failed
precondition keeps every snapshot unpublished and reports a stable diagnostic.
Already-published snapshots cannot be written, mapped, republished or invalidated
by subsequent native resource loss. Independent snapshots retain only original
CPU Request/layout/Result quota and immutable bytes, never State, root, native
closure/allocation, Recording/Submission or loss subscription. Caller shell drop
while submitted still cancels/quarantines; it must not publish from future output.
